how does all this look?

Thought for 2m 6s
It looks promising overall. The big strength is that your prompts are trying to be scholarly, restrained, and contradiction-aware rather than “cool fantasy concept art.” The Archives set in particular has a solid backbone: plain black-and-white plan style, explicit “canon / implied / reconstructed” separation, and repeated instructions not to improvise when the evidence is thin. That’s the right instinct. 

 


The best part is your control language. Lines like “do not invent,” “keep uncertain areas minimal,” “preserve previously approved entrances, corridors, stairs, and adjacent rooms,” and “reject the detail rather than improvising” are exactly the kind of constraints that stop image models from drifting into nonsense. That makes these much stronger than a normal fantasy floor-plan prompt. 

 

 


The first thing I’d fix is the Archives height. Your Archives prompts and floor-plan briefs currently lock the building as five-story, but the canon text says the Archives is six stories tall and also extends underground. If you leave “five-story” in, you’re baking a contradiction into every generated plan before the model even starts. 

 

 

 


The second thing I’d fix is the lighting language in the Archives prompt. In one place you say no windows anywhere in the building, but elsewhere you ask for “atmospheric lighting: natural light through windows” and then explicitly tell the model to make Tomes “well-lit with natural light … imply clerestory or other means.” That is exactly the sort of contradiction that will make an image model quietly cheat on the canon. 

 


The third issue is that the Archives floor prompts for the 2nd, 3rd, 4th, and 5th floors are basically the same brief pasted four times. That means the model has almost no reason to produce genuinely different floors. It will tend to give you four near-duplicates with minor noise. You need floor-specific constraints such as: ground floor must carry the entry threshold and Tomes relationship; upper floors are mostly Stacks districts; one upper level may privilege scriv-work zones if you want that as reconstructed; top occupied floor should reserve the locked roof access route. Right now the prompts are too uniform. 

 

 

 


The weakest set is the non-Archives building prompts. The Bursar prompt reads generic and misses the best actual spatial evidence: Lorren leads Kvothe across a courtyard, into another stone building, through a hallway, and down a flight of stairs to the bursar. Your current bursar brief replaces that with vague admin-language like “central location,” “reception,” and “service areas,” which may be plausible, but they are not the strongest canon anchors. 

 


The Crucible prompt is even shakier. In the material here, I can see evidence for the Crucible as a place/classroom/lab context with crucibles, hot iron pans, and a water canister in the room, but your brief inflates that into a fairly definite standalone sympathy laboratory building with secure zones, ventilation systems, and dedicated circulation. That may be a useful reconstruction direction, but it is not as canon-secure as the Archives set. 

 

 


The Fishery / Artificery prompt also wants tightening. The canon snippets in front of me support a real Fishery workshop, damageable shop quarters, and Kilvin’s office inside or off that workshop, but your current prompt blurs “Fishery” and “Artificery” into a workshop complex and adds a firm “roof exit” / adjacency logic that feels more asserted than evidenced in the files you’ve shown here. That one needs another pass from the ledger before I’d trust it as a generator prompt. 

 

 


So my honest read is: Archives = close, with 2–3 important fixes. Bursar / Crucible / Fishery = not rubbish, but still too generic or too assertive in places. If you want a blunt score, I’d say the Archives prompt family is around 8/10 after corrections, while the other building prompts are more like 5.5–6.5/10 because they don’t yet feel as tightly moored to the canon evidence. 

 

 

 


The next smart move would be for me to rewrite these prompts into a clean “generation-ready v2” set, starting with the Archives and then rebuilding the weaker ones so they only claim what the ledger actually supports.

give specific feedback on everything in here so codex and copilot can improve it, give them a great prompt to make everything 100 percent accurate and internally consistent

Thought for 3m 12s
It’s good bones, not finished discipline.

The project already has the right overall philosophy: freeze evidence first, then campus, then buildings, then floors, then images. That is exactly the right workflow for this kind of reconstruction. The Archives prompts also already push in the right direction: technical drawing style, hard bans, consistency requirements, and explicit “reject the detail rather than improvising.” 

 


The main problem is that the repo is still mixing canon, reasonable inference, and flat invention in a way that will absolutely make Codex, Copilot, Gemini, or Nano Banana drift. The fix is not “make the prompts prettier.” The fix is: one source of truth, one certainty system, one validation pass, and zero uncited hard-coded facts.

Also, one honest note: you can make this 100% accurate to the evidence you have, but not 100% complete/resolved, because the books themselves leave some things open. So the target should be: maximally canon-faithful, internally consistent, and explicit about unknowns. That is achievable. 

 


Specific feedback on the whole project
1. Your highest-level workflow is solid
Keep:

evidence ledger first

campus freeze second

building briefs third

floorplan schema before image generation

image prompts last

That logic in the project is good and should stay. 


2. The repo still has contradictory “truths”
This is the biggest issue.

The clearest example is the Archives height. Your current uploaded Archives prompt now says six-story in places, which is closer to the canon-supported direction, but other project files still hard-code five-story. Your ground, 3rd, 4th, and 5th Archives floor prompts all still say “five-story square grey windowless monolithic block,” while the current separate archives prompt and site-plan file say six-story. That means the repo currently has two incompatible shells for the same building. 

 

 

 

 

 


You need a single machine-readable canonical shell definition for each building, and every downstream prompt must inherit from it instead of retyping facts manually.

3. Some prompts directly contradict canon lighting / envelope logic
Your current archives-prompt.md still says:

base style includes “natural light through windows”

Tomes should be “well-lit with natural light … imply clerestory or other means”

But the same prompt also says the Archives is windowless. That is a direct contradiction inside the same prompt family. Tomes should be described as well-lit by sympathy lamps / controlled artificial light, not natural window light. The canon text supports the Archives being windowless and Tomes being well-lit with sympathy lamps. 

 

 


4. Some prompts are still too generic and detached from the actual evidence
The Bursar office prompt is the best example. Right now it reads like a generic admin-office prompt: central location, reception, clerical workspaces, secure document storage. But the strongest actual canon anchor is much simpler and much better: Lorren leads Kvothe across a courtyard, into another stone building, through a hallway, and down a flight of stairs to the bursar. That sequence should be the backbone of the brief. 

 


So:

keep “administrative office for finances”

remove generic admin filler unless tagged inferred

anchor the prompt around the courtyard → stone building → hallway → stair down → bursar sequence

5. Hollows is overcommitted in places
Canon strongly supports:

Hollows is simple and square

has stained-glass windows

reached from Archives by down, then left

identifiable by two big maples

contains the admissions theatre

Elodin has an office there 

 

 


What is not locked cleanly is:

whether Elodin’s lecture hall is definitely in Hollows

the exact relationship between Hollows and Masters’ Hall

So Codex/Copilot should:

keep Elodin’s office in Hollows as canon

mark Elodin’s lecture hall in Hollows as uncertain / unsupported unless separately justified

preserve the Hollows / Masters’ Hall relationship as unresolved unless the ledger explicitly freezes a bridge

6. The campus still overclaims some adjacencies and directions
Canon gives you some excellent anchors:

University is west of Imre

Imre is a little over two miles from the University on the eastern side of the Omethi

Archives visible from the bridge approach

Hollows reached from Archives by down, then left

Mews radial with eight wings

Mains sprawling and irregular 

 

 

 


But you still have unresolved campus-wide relationships, especially:

exact global compass placement of many buildings

whether the “small city/town” around the University is simply Imre or a distinct University-serving settlement in description

exact position of Masters’ Hall

So do not hard-code cardinal neighbours like “Mains west / Archives east / Medica south / courtyard west” unless the ledger really supports them. The current project still looks prone to that sort of overclaiming.

7. Archives floor prompts are too duplicated
Your current Archives ground / 3rd / 4th / 5th prompts are nearly the same prompt copied four times. That means the model has no real reason to produce meaningfully different floors. 

 

 

 


Each floor prompt should inherit the shared shell, then add floor-specific obligations:

ground: public threshold, antechamber, Tomes relationship, front-of-house control

lower/stack floors: denser storage, maze logic, service zones

one floor may host more scriv-processing zones if you explicitly mark that reconstructed

top occupied floor: roof-access route and upper Stack condition

basement / underground if included: only if clearly distinguished as underground / subgrade and not confused with the six stories above

8. Tomes / Stacks access logic needs to be stricter everywhere
Canon is very clear on this:

Tomes is the main reading area

the Stacks are Arcanum only

stack access has a separate stack fee

Archives access is ledger-controlled / “in the book”

books do not leave the Archives 

 

 


So every relevant prompt and JSON spec should encode:

Tomes ≠ Stacks

entry hall / desk control matters

not all students browse all books

book request system exists

nothing leaves the Archives

9. Mews needs tighter contradiction handling
What is strong:

circular hub

eight wings

three-story external description

fourth-floor east-wing bunk tension

ground-floor bathing facilities 


Your current direction of reconciling that as “three main exterior storeys plus partial upper level / attic-like fourth-floor accommodation” is good as a frozen bridge, but it must be marked as such everywhere, not drift between files as if it were canon. 


10. Mains should be dirtier and less rational everywhere
The strongest Mains canon is excellent:

nearly an acre and a half

grew in all directions

engulfed smaller buildings and courtyards

odd turns, dead ends, shortcuts

isolated courtyard accessible through a window

theatre-like classroom

messy roofscape of chimneys and mixed materials 


So anything in the repo that makes Mains neat, orthogonal, or centrally rational needs to be stripped back.

11. Fishery / Artificery / Crucible need a harder evidence filter
The current uploaded fishery-artificery-site-plan.md still reads too assertively in places: “roof exit,” “ventilation/emergency,” “near water-related facilities,” etc. Some of that may be a reasonable design move, but it is not equally strong canon. 


Similarly, the Crucible tends to become a generic “sympathy lab building” too quickly unless you anchor it to the actual classroom/work evidence and mark everything else inferred.

Rule for these:

separate canon function

from workable reconstruction

from invented systems language

If you must add ventilation / safety zoning / room groupings, label them [reconstructed].

12. The repo needs a real validation layer
Right now you have philosophy, but you need enforcement.

You need a validator that catches things like:

Archives = both five and six stories

windowless building + natural-light-through-windows prompt

Elodin office in Hollows vs Elodin lecture hall in unidentified building

Masters’ Hall treated as both unresolved and fully specified

unsupported exact dimensions / entrances / cardinal adjacencies

prompts that mention features absent from the frozen brief

Without that, you’ll keep reintroducing contradictions every time a prompt gets revised.

What Codex and Copilot should change, specifically
Tell them to do all of this:

Create one canonical data file for the whole project, preferably canon_manifest.yaml or canon_manifest.json, with:

entity_id

entity_type

claim

certainty = canon | implied | reconstructed | unknown | tension

source_ref

notes

hard_ban boolean

frozen_bridge boolean

Refactor all prompts to inherit from the manifest, not from manually copied prose blocks.

Delete duplicated prompt facts and replace them with template placeholders pulled from the manifest.

Add a validation script that fails the build if:

two files give incompatible story counts

a hard ban is violated

a reconstructed claim is presented as canon

a child prompt includes a fact not present in parent freeze files

a prompt introduces windows into the Archives

a prompt introduces natural light into windowless spaces without explicit canonical support

Split every building file into five sections only:

Canon

Strong implication

Reconstructed bridge

Unknowns

Hard bans

Force floor-specific prompts instead of cloned floor prompts.

Add a repo-wide terminology file:

whether “Fishery” and “Artificery” are treated as same complex or adjacent spaces

whether “House of the Wind / Pennant Square / Questioning Hall” is frozen as one working square

whether “Belows / Billows” is one zone with spelling uncertainty

what “north” means across every plan

Make every unresolved issue explicit, not silent.

Remove or soften unsupported details like exact dimensions, exact entrance counts, exact cardinal neighbours, and exact floor allocations unless ledger-backed.

Make the source hierarchy machine-enforced:

PDFs / ledger first

campus freeze second

building briefs third

floorplan JSON fourth

AI prompts last

Great prompt for Codex / Copilot
Use this as your master refactor prompt:

You are refactoring a canon-constrained architectural reconstruction repo for the University from Patrick Rothfuss’s Kingkiller Chronicle.

Your goal is NOT to make the project prettier.
Your goal is to make it maximally faithful to the current evidence base, internally consistent, machine-checkable, and safe for downstream use by image models and code assistants.

PROJECT GOAL
Make the repo 100% accurate to the frozen evidence that exists in the project, while preserving unresolved canon questions as unresolved.
Do NOT “solve” ambiguity by invention.
Do NOT add specificity unless the evidence or a clearly marked frozen bridge supports it.

SOURCE HIERARCHY (highest wins)
1. lawful-text evidence and evidence-ledger rows
2. campus-layout freeze
3. building briefs / reconstruction briefs
4. floorplan JSON
5. AI prompts

If lower-level files contradict higher-level files, lower-level files must be rewritten.
If evidence itself contains tension, preserve the tension and mark it explicitly.

PRIMARY TASKS
1. Audit the entire repo for contradictions, unsupported claims, duplicated facts, and drift.
2. Create a single canonical machine-readable source-of-truth file:
   - canon_manifest.yaml or canon_manifest.json
3. Move all hard factual claims into that manifest.
4. Refactor all prompts, briefs, and JSON outputs so they derive from the manifest rather than hard-coded repeated prose.
5. Add a validation script that fails on contradictions and hard-ban violations.
6. Produce a concise audit report of every change.

REQUIRED CERTAINTY TAGS
Every claim must be tagged as one of:
- canon
- implied
- reconstructed
- unknown
- tension

DEFINITIONS
- canon = directly supported by evidence ledger / source text
- implied = strongly supported but not directly stated
- reconstructed = practical bridge introduced to make a plan usable
- unknown = unresolved and must stay unresolved
- tension = two pieces of project evidence conflict and neither can be silently discarded

HARD RULES
- Do not invent exact dimensions unless canon supplies them.
- Do not invent entrances, windows, stairs, towers, wings, courtyards, or adjacencies to make plans feel complete.
- Do not convert reconstructed claims into canon wording.
- Do not suppress contradictions.
- Do not let child prompts override parent freezes.
- Do not let image prompts include facts absent from the frozen brief.

KNOWN ISSUES TO FIX
1. Archives shell contradiction:
   - some files say five-story
   - some files say six-story
   - resolve repo-wide using the evidence base and propagate consistently
2. Archives lighting contradiction:
   - some prompts say windowless
   - some prompts still ask for natural light through windows / clerestory
   - remove this contradiction
3. Archives floor prompts are over-duplicated
   - make each floor prompt floor-specific
4. Bursar prompt is too generic
   - anchor it to actual evidence sequence: courtyard -> another stone building -> hallway -> stair down -> bursar
5. Hollows is overcommitted
   - keep Elodin’s office in Hollows if evidence supports it
   - do not force Elodin’s lecture hall into Hollows unless frozen explicitly as reconstructed
   - preserve Masters’ Hall relationship uncertainty unless evidence resolves it
6. Fishery / Artificery / Crucible prompts contain over-assertive workshop logic
   - reduce unsupported specificity
   - clearly tag reconstructed safety / ventilation / room-zoning language
7. Campus adjacencies are overclaimed in places
   - remove unsupported cardinal relationships
8. Mews contradiction handling must be explicit
   - preserve three-storey exterior vs fourth-floor east-wing tension as a frozen bridge, not silent canon
9. Mains must remain irregular and accreted everywhere

Generate this prompt for nano banana to be way more detailed please and specific and consistent with every other prompt. I have attached 10 for more context

# University Campus Layout Freeze (campus_v1)

Source of truth for this freeze: university-evidence-ledger-v3.csv.

## Locked Canon
- The University lies west of Imre and is reached by crossing Stonebridge over the Omethi canyon.
- The built environment around the University is town-sized, orderly, and service-oriented rather than a tiny isolated cloister.
- The campus has about fifteen architecturally varied buildings and supports over a thousand students.
- Admissions is a major outdoor logistical event lasting eleven days, with lines and milling crowds in a large open square or courtyard.
- The Archives are the strongest exterior anchor: a five-story square grey windowless monolith visible from Stonebridge.
- Mains is the oldest and most spatially chaotic academic core: accreted, sprawling, and full of absorbed spaces.
- Hollows is a small square stained-glass building near the Archives and is reached from the Archives by going down and then left.
- The Medica is a large irregular building and is encountered opposite or beyond the Archives from one known approach.
- The Fishery or Artificery is an industrial complex, not a single room, with a south exit toward a courtyard and low windows on its east wall.
- The Mews are radial and many-windowed, with a central hub and eight wings; the Mess lies across a lawn to the south.
- Haven is outside the dense academic core to the north, approached by road, fenced, and much more like a formal manor-institution than a prison block.
- The Underthing is a large multi-zone hidden network connected to the Archives and accessible from surface grates.

## Working Campus Logic
- Stonebridge is the eastern chokepoint and the Archives are the first major visual landmark on the University side.
- The core campus should be treated as a loose academic cluster with roads, lawns, gardens, and service circulation between buildings rather than wall-to-wall density.
- Mains functions as the central academic labyrinth.
- Archives and Hollows form the most strongly evidenced eastern administrative or ceremonial cluster.
- Medica and Fishery belong on the productive or service-heavy side of campus rather than inside the ceremonial core.
- Mews and Mess belong together in the residential belt, with Mess south of Mews across open lawn.
- Haven should stay isolated from the student core and read as its own secured compound at the north edge.

## Frozen Design Decisions
- Treat House of the Wind, Pennant Square, and the whipping courtyard as one working central square until later evidence forces a split. Keep the alternate naming in notes.
- Treat Belows and earlier Billows as the same deep wind-tunnel Underthing zone, with spelling uncertainty preserved in notes.
- Reconcile the Mews height tension by treating the building as three full exterior stories with at least one partial upper level or attic-like fourth-floor accommodation in the east wing.
- Keep project north as map-up for consistency, but treat most campus-wide compass placement outside the Imre/Stonebridge/University axis as reconstructed rather than proven.

## Adjacency Spine
- Imre -> Stonebridge -> University approach.
- Stonebridge -> sightline to the Archives.
- the Archives -> near Hollows; opposite or beyond the Medica from one courtyard approach; connected to the Underthing.
- Hollows -> near the Archives; contains the admissions theatre and Elodin's office.
- the Mews -> the Mess across lawn to the south.
- the Fishery -> south-facing exit toward a courtyard; likely tied to the working-yard side of campus.
- Mains roof -> courtyard edge via apple tree.
- Haven -> north road, outside dense campus core.

## Open Questions
- Exact compass placement of Mains, Mews, Fishery, Crucible, Ledgers and Lists, and Masters' Hall remains partly reconstructed.
- The exact relation between House of the Wind, Pennant Square, and Questioning Hall is still a naming problem, not a solved canon fact.
- The exact siting of Masters' Hall and the Bursar relative to Hollows and the central square is still incomplete.
- The number and placement of Underthing access points beyond the Archives are still underdetermined.

## Prompt-Ready Campus Brief
Large medieval university west of Imre across Stonebridge, embedded in an orderly town-sized settlement with roads, lawns, gardens, and specialist shops. Use the Archives as the eastern visual monolith, Mains as the sprawling central labyrinth, Hollows as the nearby ceremonial stained-glass building, Medica and Fishery as large working institutions, Mews and Mess as the residential cluster, and Haven as a separate northern secured manor-compound. Keep the central cobblestone gathering square large enough for admissions queues and public punishments, and preserve uncertainty where canon does not lock exact compass placement.

formal and substantial

distinct from prison, dungeon, or military barracks

isolated enough to feel psychologically separate from student life

Include one large central square / courtyard that can plausibly serve as:

large

What percentage accurate is this 

Thought for 1m 45s
I’d call it about 72% accurate overall as a campus freeze.

That’s fairly good, especially for a first anchor image. The big-picture campus logic is mostly right: the Imre → Stonebridge → University axis reads clearly, the Archives are the eastern visual anchor, Hollows sits near the Archives, Mains is central, Mews and Mess have the right south-across-lawn relationship, and Haven is isolated to the north as its own fenced compound. Those are the biggest wins.

What pulls the score down is that it over-resolves uncertainty. Your own freeze says several campus-wide placements are still partly reconstructed, including the exact siting of buildings like Mains, Fishery, Crucible, Ledgers and Lists, and Masters’ Hall, and that most campus-wide compass placement outside the Imre/Stonebridge axis should stay cautious. But the drawing presents most of the campus as if it were already cleanly settled. That makes it feel more certain than your evidence freeze actually allows. 


The largest specific problem is the Archives exterior logic. The current drawing makes the Archives read too symmetrically and too generically accessible. Your Archives prompt family is much stricter: the building should have one public entrance only, it should not be on the most obvious approach face, and the exterior should stay severe, blank, and unornamented. In this image, the Archives footprint reads as if it has repeated mid-face protrusions or equalised face treatment, which weakens the “single hard-to-find entrance” idea. That’s the main reason I wouldn’t rate it higher.

A second issue is Mains. It is better than a neat quadrangle, but it still looks a bit too composed and legible. Your Mains brief wants something that feels cobbled together from mismatched pieces, like it swallowed older buildings and courtyards, with no symmetry and no singular unified facade. The current version hints at that, but it still feels more “designed irregular” than “grown over centuries.” 


The Fishery / Artificery placement is plausible, but still a bit too clean. The brief wants it to read as an industrial working complex with working-yard logic, east-wall window logic, and a south exit toward a courtyard. In the image it is in roughly the right belt of campus, but it still reads more like a tidy block than a practical workshop complex. 


The best parts to keep are:

the east-west regional spine

the size and usefulness of the central square

Haven’s separation to the north

the Mews/Mess relationship

the general town-scale openness with roads, lawns, and breathing room

Those parts are doing the right job.

The next fixes I’d make are:

redraw the Archives shell so it has one clearly marked reconstructed entrance face only, and make the other faces plainer

make Mains messier and less balanced

mark more campus placements as reconstructed instead of letting them all read as locked canon

make the Fishery feel more like a workshop yard complex

clean up or remove the long right-hand note block, because the garbled text weakens the scholarly feel even where the geometry is decent

So, bluntly:

Macro campus logic: 84%

Canon uncertainty handling: 55%

Building-specific fidelity: 68%

Overall weighted score: 72%

If you fix the Archives entrance logic and stop overcommitting uncertain building placements, it could jump into the 82–86% range pretty quickly.


Write an extensive highly detailed prompt to make it as accurate and specific as possible to the books

Thought for 57s
I tightened this around the strongest book-supported campus anchors: the University is west of Imre across Stonebridge and the Omethi, it sits in a town-like service settlement with roads, lawns, and specialist shops, the campus has about fifteen varied buildings, the Archives are the eastern severe monolith, Mains is the sprawling accreted core, Hollows is small/square/stained-glass, Mews is radial, and Haven is isolated to the north. I’ve also kept the unresolved placements genuinely unresolved instead of pretending the whole campus is fully solved.

Use this as your master campus prompt:

Create a **strictly top-down, orthographic architectural site plan** of the **University campus and its immediate settlement context** from Patrick Rothfuss’s *Kingkiller Chronicle* as a **canon-constrained reconstruction**.

This image must function as the **master campus layout freeze** for the whole project.
It is not fantasy concept art.
It is not a decorative map.
It is not a cinematic establishing shot.
It is not a full regional map.
It is not a narrative illustration.

It must be drawn like a **measured, restrained, scholarly planning document** that another architect or reconstruction artist could use as a base reference for later building site plans and floor plans.

## CORE INTENT
The goal is to reconstruct the University campus as faithfully as possible to the books while preserving uncertainty where the books do not fully resolve it.

The drawing must:
- prioritize canon-supported spatial relationships
- prefer modest, institutional, believable planning over spectacle
- show only enough settlement context to orient the campus properly
- avoid over-inventing exact building placements where the books remain ambiguous
- make uncertain placements legible as `reconstructed`, not silently “solved”

## DRAWING MODE
Use:
- true top-down orthographic view
- black-and-white architectural site plan graphics
- clean linework
- restrained hatch only if useful
- north arrow
- scale bar
- simple labels
- plain legend/key
- a short notes block if needed for unresolved naming or placement issues

Do not use:
- perspective
- isometric projection
- dramatic shadows
- fantasy decoration
- parchment textures
- characters
- carts
- punishments
- admissions crowds
- heraldry
- decorative borders
- atmospheric ruins
- cinematic canyon rendering
- painterly effects

## CERTAINTY SYSTEM
Use a visible plain key with:
- `canon`
- `implied`
- `reconstructed`

Apply these consistently:
- `canon` = directly supported by the books
- `implied` = strong spatial inference from the books
- `reconstructed` = conservative working placement chosen to make the campus usable and coherent

Do not present a reconstructed placement as if it were a proven canon fact.

## OVERALL CAMPUS IDENTITY
The University must read as:
- west of Imre
- reached by crossing Stonebridge over the Omethi canyon
- embedded in an orderly, service-oriented, town-scale settlement
- academically institutional rather than aristocratic
- varied in architecture rather than master-planned uniformity
- large enough to support over a thousand students
- spread out with roads, paths, lawns, gardens, courtyards, and working yards
- not a tiny isolated cloister
- not a walled magical citadel
- not a palace complex
- not a monastery quadrangle
- not a fortress keep with decorative additions

The campus should feel like a **lived-in university town cluster**, not a ceremonial capital.

## REGIONAL SPATIAL SPINE — MUST BE CLEAR
The east-west approach must be the clearest campus-level spatial fact in the drawing:

- Imre lies to the **east**
- Stonebridge crosses the Omethi canyon
- the University lies to the **west** of that crossing
- the Archives are the first major severe academic landmark on the University side

This axis must be legible immediately from the plan.

Do not bury or confuse this relationship.

## SETTLEMENT CONTEXT
Show enough context to make the campus believable as part of a service town:
- a limited amount of Imre-side or settlement-side street structure
- roads and lanes
- some small surrounding buildings or blocks at the edges where helpful
- the sense of a larger settlement serving the University
- open ground, lawns, and gardens around the main academic buildings

Do not:
- fully map all of Imre
- draw every surrounding house
- over-detail the service town
- invent a dense complete city plan
- turn the University into an isolated island of buildings in empty landscape

The books support a **town-sized, orderly, service-oriented built environment**, not a tiny cloister or an empty field campus.

## CAMPUS-WIDE COMPOSITION
Lay out the campus as a **loose academic cluster** with distinct functional belts:

1. **Eastern threshold / ceremonial / administrative cluster**
2. **Central academic core**
3. **Productive / service-heavy zone**
4. **Residential belt**
5. **Northern isolated secure compound**

These zones should be evident in planning logic even where exact building coordinates remain reconstructed.

## EASTERN THRESHOLD / CEREMONIAL CLUSTER
This part of campus must include:
- the Archives as the strongest eastern monolithic anchor
- Hollows near the Archives
- a large central square or courtyard suitable for admissions logistics and public punishment
- practical approach paths or roads from the Stonebridge side
- enough openness that the Archives remain a dominant visual exterior anchor

This zone should feel:
- formal
- exposed
- institutional
- public-facing
- academic
- not festive or decorative

Do not turn it into:
- a palace forecourt
- a cathedral close
- a ceremonial parade ground
- a tiny enclosed cloister court

## CENTRAL ACADEMIC CORE
Mains must sit as the central academic labyrinth:
- old
- sprawling
- accreted
- chaotic
- composed of mismatched pieces
- suggestive of swallowed buildings and courtyards
- visibly less rational than the rest of the campus

Mains must **not** read as:
- symmetrical
- cleanly axial
- neatly rectangular
- a classic quadrangle
- one unified facade mass

It should feel like the campus’s oldest academic body, grown over centuries.

## PRODUCTIVE / SERVICE SIDE
The Medica and Fishery / Artificery belong on the more practical side of campus:
- not in the ceremonial threshold cluster
- not hidden like tiny annexes
- not treated as decorative landmarks

### Medica
The Medica should read as:
- large
- irregular
- substantial
- institutional
- active
- more practical than ceremonial
- a serious medical / service building

### Fishery / Artificery
The Fishery / Artificery should read as:
- industrial
- workshop-based
- practical
- tied to working-yard logic
- not residential
- not office-like
- not a mere classroom block

Its placement should plausibly support:
- east-wall light logic
- a south exit toward a courtyard
- service / work circulation

Do not invent excessive substructures or unsupported annexes.

## RESIDENTIAL BELT
The residential cluster must include:
- Mews
- Mess

### Mews
Mews must read as:
- radial
- many-windowed
- a central hub with eight wings
- clearly residential
- larger than a simple dormitory
- practical rather than ornate

### Mess
Mess must be:
- south of Mews
- across open lawn
- separate from Mews
- simpler and more hall-like than the major academic buildings

This Mews -> lawn -> Mess south relationship must be preserved clearly.

## NORTHERN SECURED COMPOUND
Haven must be:
- to the north of the dense academic core
- reached by road
- fenced
- spatially distinct
- manor-institutional rather than prison-block
- isolated enough to feel separate from ordinary student circulation

It should read as a dedicated secure institutional precinct, not merely another academic hall.

## CENTRAL SQUARE / COURTYARD
Include one large open square / courtyard that can plausibly serve as:
- admissions queues
- student milling
- public punishments
- a major campus gathering space

Treat:
- House of the Wind
- Pennant Square
- the whipping courtyard

as one working central square for this freeze.

Do not split them into separate spaces unless explicitly forced by evidence.
Keep any naming uncertainty in a small notes block, not by generating multiple separate squares.

This square must be:
- large
- open
- practical
- institutional
- important to circulation
- not overly landscaped
- not ceremonial in a noble sense
- not tiny

## THE ARCHIVES — EXTERIOR CAMPUS ROLE
The Archives must read as:
- the strongest eastern visual anchor
- austere
- square in footprint
- windowless
- severe
- blank-faced
- monolithic
- more forbidding than inviting
- externally simpler than its interior complexity suggests

Do not:
- add windows
- add extra exterior entrances
- ornament it
- give it decorative towers
- make it look like a palace, church, or fortress keep
- place it so deep in the cluster that it stops functioning as the eastern anchor

If the approved shell elsewhere already fixes the exact height, use that shell.
If not, do **not** hard-code a conflicting storey count here; just draw it as a tall severe square monolith.

## HOLLOWS
Hollows must read as:
- smaller than the Archives and Mains
- square or nearly square
- simple
- near the Archives
- part of the eastern cluster
- associated with stained-glass identity

Place Hollows conservatively consistent with the known relation:
from the Archives, it is reached by going “down, then left.”

Do not overstate this into over-precise compass geometry beyond what the master campus orientation supports.

## MEDICA
The Medica must read as:
- large
- irregular
- substantial
- institutional
- medical/service-oriented rather than ceremonial
- opposite or beyond the Archives from one known courtyard approach

Use that relationship conservatively without pretending its full global compass placement is fully solved.

## FISHERY / ARTIFICERY
The Fishery / Artificery must read as:
- an industrial working complex
- not just a small hall
- on the practical / productive side of campus
- plausibly tied to working-yard space
- not tucked into the ceremonial eastern cluster
- not merged into Mains
- not residential

Keep its exact final placement cautious if not fully locked.

## MEWS
The Mews must:
- preserve the radial hub-and-eight-wings geometry
- feel residential and many-windowed
- sit in the residential belt
- pair clearly with the Mess to the south across lawn

Do not stylize the Mews into an ornamental flower-like fantasy building.

## HAVEN
Haven must:
- be clearly north
- outside the dense student core
- be road-connected
- be fenced
- read as formal, substantial, and controlled
- not read as dungeon-maze architecture from above

Its separation from the main student core must be obvious.

## UNDERTHING SURFACE LOGIC
The Underthing is large and important but mostly hidden.

At the campus site-plan level:
- do not try to map the Underthing fully
- do not create a dramatic underground cutaway
- do not invent extensive exterior vent towers
- do not overpopulate the surface with unexplained grates

At most, show:
- very minimal conservative service / ventilation logic
- only if needed
- only where it helps explain Archives connection
- only if it stays understated

The Underthing should remain mostly invisible at this scale.

## ROADS, PATHS, LAWNS, AND GARDENS
The campus must include:
- roads
- connecting paths
- lawns
- some gardens
- open ground between buildings
- believable circulation between academic, residential, industrial, and secure zones

Do not:
- make the campus wall-to-wall stone
- eliminate outdoor breathing room
- turn all open space into decorative gardens
- make circulation purely symbolic rather than practical

## TOPOGRAPHY
Show the Omethi canyon and Stonebridge clearly enough that:
- the crossing matters
- the eastern approach makes sense
- the Archives can plausibly be seen as the first strong visual landmark on the University side

But do not:
- turn the canyon into a dramatic fantasy ravine illustration
- over-render cliffs for spectacle
- make the whole plan about the canyon

Keep topography restrained and diagrammatic.

## UNCERTAINTY HANDLING
Where the books do not fully lock placement:
- choose the most conservative believable planning arrangement
- mark it `reconstructed`
- keep footprints modest
- avoid over-precise compass claims
- preserve unresolved relationships as unresolved

This especially applies to:
- exact placement of Mains relative to some secondary buildings
- exact siting of Fishery / Crucible / Ledgers and Lists / Masters’ Hall
- exact global placement of some buildings beyond the Imre -> Stonebridge -> University axis
- number and placement of Underthing access points

Do not smooth over uncertainty with elegant symmetry.

## GRAPHIC CONVENTIONS
Use:
- strong lineweight for primary canon anchors
- lighter lineweight for reconstructed masses
- restrained hatching or fill only if helpful
- simple room/building labels
- plain legend
- north arrow
- scale bar
- optional notes block for unresolved naming or placement tensions

- Imre

- decorative fantasy architecture
- extra towers for silhouette

- full campus walls or fortifications unless explicitly frozen elsewhere
- ornamental cloisters

- multiple invented Underthing access points
- unsupported exact dimensions
- over-resolved cardinal placement of every building
- decorative water features unless explicitly required
- people, processions, wagons, punishments, banners, or scene elements

## FINAL IMAGE CHARACTER
The final drawing should feel like:
- a serious reconstruction

It should not feel like:

- not just a small hall
- on the practical / productive side of campus
- plausibly tied to working-yard space
- not tucked into the ceremonial eastern cluster
- not merged into Mains
- not residential

Keep its exact final placement cautious if not fully locked.

## MEWS
The Mews must:
- preserve the radial hub-and-eight-wings geometry
- feel residential and many-windowed
- sit in the residential belt
- pair clearly with the Mess to the south across lawn

Do not stylize the Mews into an ornamental flower-like fantasy building.

## HAVEN
Haven must:
- be clearly north
- outside the dense student core
- be road-connected
- be fenced
- read as formal, substantial, and controlled
- not read as dungeon-maze architecture from above

Its separation from the main student core must be obvious.

## UNDERTHING SURFACE LOGIC
The Underthing is large and important but mostly hidden.

At the campus site-plan level:
- do not try to map the Underthing fully
- do not create a dramatic underground cutaway
- do not invent extensive exterior vent towers
- do not overpopulate the surface with unexplained grates

At most, show:
- very minimal conservative service / ventilation logic
- only if needed
- only where it helps explain Archives connection
- only if it stays understated

The Underthing should remain mostly invisible at this scale.

## ROADS, PATHS, LAWNS, AND GARDENS
The campus must include:
- roads
- connecting paths
- lawns
- some gardens
- open ground between buildings
- believable circulation between academic, residential, industrial, and secure zones

Do not:
- make the campus wall-to-wall stone
- eliminate outdoor breathing room
- turn all open space into decorative gardens
- make circulation purely symbolic rather than practical

## TOPOGRAPHY
Show the Omethi canyon and Stonebridge clearly enough that:
- the crossing matters
- the eastern approach makes sense
- the Archives can plausibly be seen as the first strong visual landmark on the University side

But do not:
- turn the canyon into a dramatic fantasy ravine illustration
- over-render cliffs for spectacle
- make the whole plan about the canyon

Keep topography restrained and diagrammatic.

## UNCERTAINTY HANDLING
Where the books do not fully lock placement:
- choose the most conservative believable planning arrangement
- mark it reconstructed
- keep footprints modest
- avoid over-precise compass claims
- preserve unresolved relationships as unresolved

This especially applies to:
- exact placement of Mains relative to some secondary buildings
- exact siting of Fishery / Crucible / Ledgers and Lists / Masters’ Hall
- exact global placement of some buildings beyond the Imre -> Stonebridge -> University axis
- number and placement of Underthing access points

Do not smooth over uncertainty with elegant symmetry.

## GRAPHIC CONVENTIONS
Use:
- strong lineweight for primary canon anchors
- lighter lineweight for reconstructed masses
- restrained hatching or fill only if helpful
- simple room/building labels
- plain legend
- north arrow
- scale bar
- optional notes block for unresolved naming or placement tensions

Suggested labels:
- Imre
- Stonebridge
- Omethi canyon
- Archives
- Hollows
- Mains
- Medica
- Fishery / Artificery
- Mews
- Mess
- Haven
- Central Square / Courtyard

Do not over-label speculative items.

## HARD BANS
Absolutely do not include:
- decorative fantasy architecture
- extra towers for silhouette
- giant ceremonial stairs
- palace courtyards
- cathedral forecourts
- full campus walls or fortifications unless explicitly frozen elsewhere
- ornamental cloisters
- over-detailed service-town mapping
- multiple invented Underthing access points
- unsupported exact dimensions
- over-resolved cardinal placement of every building
- decorative water features unless explicitly required
- people, processions, wagons, punishments, banners, or scene elements

## FINAL IMAGE CHARACTER
The final drawing should feel like:
- a serious reconstruction
- institutional
- practical
- academically believable
- town-scaled
- restrained
- useful as a base map for later plans

It should not feel like:
- a fantasy poster
- a tourism map
- a theme-park masterplan
- a palace estate
- a solved perfect campus where every uncertainty has disappeared

## FINAL QUALITY CHECK
Before finalizing, verify all of the following:
- Imre is east
- Stonebridge is the eastern crossing
- the University lies west of the bridge
- the Archives are the eastern monolithic anchor
- Hollows is near the Archives
- Mains is central and irregular
- Medica and Fishery are on the practical/service side rather than buried inside the ceremonial threshold cluster
- Mews and Mess preserve the south-across-lawn relationship
- Haven is isolated to the north by road and fence
- the central square is large and useful
- the campus reads as a town-sized academic settlement
- uncertainty is preserved rather than silently erased
- nothing decorative or overly invented has been added

Thought for 1m 7s
Yes — and the biggest improvement is this:

Your current prompt treats the bursar like a standalone campus building, but the books give you stronger, tighter evidence than that. In The Name of the Wind, Lorren takes Kvothe across a courtyard, into another stone building, through a hallway, and down a flight of stairs to Riem. In The Wise Man’s Fear, Kvothe explicitly says he visits the bursar in the basement of Hollows, and Riem keeps his counting room there while handling tuition and University money. That means the best reconstruction target is not “a generic bursar building site plan,” but the bursar’s lower-level office suite within Hollows, plus the immediate approach sequence from courtyard to hall to stair to counting room.

So I’d replace the old prompt with this much more specific one:

Create a **strictly top-down, orthographic architectural plan** of the **Bursar’s Office suite and its immediate approach sequence within Hollows** from Patrick Rothfuss’s *Kingkiller Chronicle* as a **canon-constrained reconstruction**, not a fantasy invention.

This is **not** a full campus plan.
This is **not** a speculative standalone bursar building unless the drawing clearly shows it as part of Hollows.
This is **not** a decorative fantasy illustration.

The image must function as a **technical planning reference** for the bursar’s office area:
- the approach from the courtyard
- entry into the stone building
- the internal hallway
- the stair descent
- the lower-level bursarial space
- the counting-room / finance-room logic of Riem’s domain

## CORE INTENT
Reconstruct the bursar’s office area as faithfully as possible to the books while staying modest, practical, and institutionally believable.

The image must:
- prioritize direct book evidence
- preserve uncertainty where evidence stops
- avoid inventing unnecessary rooms or office hierarchies
- show the bursar as part of a real university administrative environment
- keep the plan restrained, clerical, and functional rather than grand or cinematic

## DRAWING MODE
Use:
- true top-down orthographic view
- black-and-white architectural plan drawing
- clear walls, doors, corridor lines, stair symbols, and room boundaries
- simple labels
- north arrow
- scale bar
- plain legend / key
- certainty categories:
  - `canon`
  - `implied`
  - `reconstructed`

Do **not** use:
- perspective
- isometric view
- dramatic shadows
- decorative fantasy art
- heraldry
- characters
- money chests spilling open
- quills, coins, books, or props unless architecturally relevant
- parchment effects
- theatrical lighting
- invented grandeur

## PRIMARY RECONSTRUCTION TARGET
The target is the **bursar’s office suite in the basement / lower level of Hollows**, with the **approach sequence from exterior courtyard to interior hallway to stair down to the counting room**.

The drawing must therefore show:
1. a portion of **Hollows exterior context sufficient to explain entry**
2. a **courtyard-side approach**
3. the **building entry point**
4. the **hallway sequence**
5. the **stair down**
6. the **lower-level bursar zone**
7. Riem’s **counting room / financial office**
8. minimal supporting clerical / storage logic only where necessary

## LOCKED CANON FACTS
Represent these as the strongest fixed facts:

- Lorren leads Kvothe **across a courtyard** to the bursar.  
- He leads him into **another stone building**.  
- They pass through **a hallway**.  
- They go **down a flight of stairs**.  
- The bursar is **Riem**.  
- Riem is an elderly or later pinch-faced financial officer who handles University money, tuition, and accounts.  
- In *The Wise Man’s Fear*, Kvothe explicitly visits **the bursar in the basement of Hollows**.  
- Riem keeps a **counting room** there.  
- The office handles tuition, payments, and University financial matters. 

## BUILDING CONTEXT
Hollows must be treated consistently with the broader project:
- stone institutional building
- small compared with Mains
- square or nearly square in the larger campus logic
- associated with formal academic and administrative use
- not a residence hall
- not a workshop
- not a decorative chapel-like structure

Only show as much of Hollows as needed to orient the bursar suite.
Do **not** attempt to draw all of Hollows unless strictly necessary.

## WHAT THE PLAN SHOULD PRIORITIZE
The plan should prioritize **circulation sequence** over decorative completeness:

### Sequence to make clear
- exterior / courtyard side
- entry door into Hollows
- short internal hall or hall segment
- stair descending to lower level
- bursar’s lower-level office zone
- counting room
- adjacent records / strongroom / clerical support only if conservatively justified

This sequence is the most important thing.
A viewer should immediately understand: **courtyard -> entry -> hallway -> stair down -> bursar**.

## SPATIAL CHARACTER
The bursar area should feel:
- clerical
- financial
- slightly cramped rather than ceremonial
- lower-level
- practical
- secure in a quiet institutional way
- old university administration, not civic grandeur
- efficient rather than elegant

It should **not** feel:
- like a bank hall
- like a noble treasury
- like a courtroom
- like a dormitory
- like a guildhall
- like a fantasy vault complex
- like a professor’s private study suite

## ROOM LOGIC
Use only conservative room logic supported by function.

### Canon / near-canon room identity
- `counting room` for Riem
- lower-level bursar office / financial room
- hallway
- stair
- courtyard approach

### Strongly implied support spaces

- `records`
- `accounts`
- `clerical work`
- `secure storage`
- `waiting`
- `service`

- a strong sense of document handling and accounting logic

But do **not** over-furnish it.

- an internal descent

- not monumental
- not winding theatrically
- not a public grand stair
- not an exterior stair

- Specialized space for sympathy experimentation
- Contains sympathy equipment and materials

## 2. What is strongly implied
- Dedicated sympathy work area
- Equipment for sympathy demonstrations
- Functional laboratory space
- Part of larger academic facility

## 3. What is weakly inferred
- Stone construction with work surfaces
- Storage for sympathy materials
- Safety considerations for sympathy work
- Instructional space for students
- Controlled environment for sympathy work

## 4. What must not be contradicted
- No absence of crucible equipment
- No lack of sympathy workspace
- No non-sympathy functions
- No inadequate safety measures
- No disconnection from sympathy education

## 5. Likely building function and circulation
The Crucible serves as sympathy laboratory with specialized equipment and workspace, functioning as experimental facility for sympathy work. Circulation includes laboratory areas, equipment rooms, and storage spaces with safety considerations for sympathy experimentation.

## 6. Likely entrances, stairs, and room zones
- Main laboratory entrance
- Sympathy work areas with crucibles
- Equipment and material storage
- Safety and ventilation systems
- Staff and instructor spaces
- Controlled experimental zones

## 7. Unknowns that must remain unknown
- Exact crucible configurations
- Specific sympathy equipment
- Safety system details
- Building size and layout
- Ventilation exact design
- Exact location on campus

## 8. Final reconstruction summary
A sympathy laboratory facility containing crucibles and specialized equipment for sympathy work. The building serves as experimental workspace with laboratory areas, equipment storage, and safety systems, providing controlled environment for sympathy experimentation and education.

Rules:
- Include only buildings and routes supported by the canon brief.
- If a building position is uncertain, place it conservatively and label it as reconstructed.
- Keep all relative relationships internally consistent.
- Do not invent extra towers, wings, courtyards, or bridges unless listed in the constraints.
- Use a plain map key with:
  canon
  implied
  reconstructed
- Keep the layout practical and believable as a functioning university complex.

Consistency requirements:
- Keep north aligned with the master campus map.
- Keep scale consistent with the approved building shell.
- Preserve previously approved entrances, corridors, stairs, and adjacent rooms.
- Do not contradict earlier approved plans.
- If a new detail would force a contradiction, reject the detail rather than improvising.

Thought for 24s
Yes — and the first fix is important:

Your current version says “site plan of the University grounds”, but the evidence you’re using is really for the Crucible and its immediate context, not for the whole campus. Also, the book evidence for the Crucible is much thinner than for the Archives or Hollows: what you strongly have is a sympathy teaching / work room context with crucibles, hot iron pans, and a water canister, plus the fact that it belongs to the University’s sympathy instruction world. So the prompt should stay tight, cautious, and anti-invention.

Here’s a much stronger version:

Create a **strictly top-down, orthographic architectural site plan** of the **Crucible and its immediate exterior context** from Patrick Rothfuss’s *Kingkiller Chronicle* as a **canon-constrained reconstruction**, not a fantasy invention.

This is **not** a full campus map.
This is **not** a decorative fantasy illustration.
This is **not** a dramatic magical laboratory concept painting.

The image must function as a **technical planning reference** for the Crucible as a University sympathy-work facility:
- a place of sympathy instruction
- a place of controlled practical work
- a place containing crucibles, heated work elements, and water for work or safety
- a modest academic / laboratory setting rather than a grand magical monument

## CORE INTENT
Reconstruct the Crucible as faithfully as possible to the available book evidence while staying restrained, practical, and believable as part of a medieval-university academic complex.

The image must:
- prioritize direct evidence first
- use strong implication only where needed for plan legibility
- avoid over-inventing specialized rooms
- avoid turning the space into a fantasy alchemy cathedral
- preserve uncertainty where the books do not lock details
- treat the Crucible as a **functional sympathy-work building or suite**, not an opportunity for spectacle

## DRAWING MODE
Use:
- true top-down orthographic view
- black-and-white architectural site-plan drawing
- clear walls, doors, circulation paths, room boundaries, and work-zone boundaries
- simple labels
- north arrow
- scale bar
- plain legend / key
- certainty categories:
  - `canon`
  - `implied`
  - `reconstructed`

Do **not** use:
- perspective
- isometric projection
- dramatic shadows
- decorative fantasy art
- glowing magical effects
- alchemical sigils
- characters
- equipment theatrically exploding
- elaborate steam or flame effects
- parchment textures
- theatrical lighting
- invented grandeur

## PRIMARY RECONSTRUCTION TARGET
The target is the **Crucible as a sympathy instruction and practical-work site**, shown as a believable academic work facility within the University.

The drawing must therefore show:
1. the **Crucible building or clearly bounded suite**
2. only the **immediate exterior context needed to orient the building**
3. one **main point of entry**
4. the **primary sympathy work zone**
5. modest supporting space for materials, water, heated work, and instruction
6. only the minimum additional room logic needed to make the plan usable

If the evidence does not support a fully independent freestanding building, the image may show the Crucible as a **clearly bounded building unit or site-planned work block** within a larger academic setting, but it must remain architecturally legible as a distinct sympathy-work place.

## LOCKED EVIDENCE TO HONOR
Represent the following as the strongest anchors:

- the space is associated with **sympathy instruction**
- the space contains or supports **crucibles**
- the space includes **hot iron pans**
- the space includes a **water canister**
- the space functions as a **classroom / lab / work environment**
- it is part of the University’s educational and practical sympathy world
- it is specialized rather than generic classroom space 

These facts should drive the reconstruction.
Do not add stronger claims than these unless clearly marked `implied` or `reconstructed`.

## WHAT THE PLAN SHOULD PRIORITIZE
The plan should prioritize **functional work logic** over decorative completeness.

Make the viewer immediately understand:
- this is a controlled sympathy work area
- it supports instruction and practical experiment
- heat, materials, and water all matter here
- it belongs to a serious academic institution
- it is practical rather than mystical

## BUILDING / SUITE CHARACTER
The Crucible should feel:
- academic
- practical
- hazardous in a controlled way
- workshop-like, but more instructional than industrial
- more technical than ceremonial
- more disciplined than dramatic
- part of a real university rather than a wizard’s sanctum

It should **not** feel:
- like a temple
- like a noble laboratory
- like an artificery factory hall unless clearly supported
- like a dungeon
- like a grand observatory
- like an alchemist’s treasure vault
- like a fantasy boss-room

## EXTERIOR CHARACTER
If shown as a distinct building, the exterior should read as:
- modest
- institutional
- stone or stone-dominant if reconstruction requires a shell
- plain
- functional
- believable beside other university buildings
- not decorative
- not towered
- not chapel-like
- not militarized

If shown as part of a larger complex, the drawing must still clearly indicate:
- the Crucible’s bounded extent
- its primary entry
- its immediate exterior circulation
- its distinction from generic surrounding rooms

## ROOM / ZONE LOGIC
Use only conservative room logic.

### Canon / near-canon zones
- `sympathy work area`
- `crucibles`
- `heated work`
- `water station`
- `instruction area`
- `entry`

### Strongly implied support zones
If needed, include only a small number of these:
- `materials`
- `storage`
- `prep`
- `service`
- `instructor`
- `work benches`
- `washing / safety`
- `fuel / heat support`

### Labeling rule
Prefer generic functional labels over invented lore labels.

Good:
- `work area`
- `crucibles`
- `heated pans`
- `water`
- `materials`
- `storage`
- `instruction`
- `service`

Avoid:
- `grand sympathy chamber`
- `arcane furnace sanctum`
- `master alchemy hall`
- `binding vault`
- `thaumic reactor`
- any ornate or modernized invented terminology

## PRIMARY WORK AREA REQUIREMENTS
The central work zone should read as:
- the main place where sympathy demonstrations or practical tasks occur
- arranged around work surfaces or controlled stations
- not huge unless scale requires it
- not cluttered like a junk workshop
- not theatrical
- clearly specialized

It may include:
- a central worktable or several controlled worktables
- crucible stations
- hot iron work surfaces
- a water canister or water point
- modest perimeter storage if restrained

Do **not** over-furnish it.

## HEAT / WATER / SAFETY LOGIC
Because the evidence includes heated iron and water, the plan should support a reasonable safety logic.

This can justify, conservatively:
- separation between main work surface and circulation
- nearby water point / canister zone
- durable floors or work surfaces
- modest service or prep space
- limited material storage
- controlled work clearances

But do **not** overbuild this into:
- a modern industrial safety suite
- elaborate vent towers
- giant furnace infrastructure
- chemical plant logic
- magical reactor architecture

## ENTRANCE AND CIRCULATION
The Crucible should have:
- one clear main entry
- simple, practical circulation
- enough room for students and instruction
- controlled movement around heated work areas

Circulation should feel:
- supervised
- efficient
- modest
- believable for repeated teaching use

Do **not** create:
- processional entry
- ceremonial stair
- heroic vestibule
- multiple decorative entrances
- grand axial composition

## STRONGLY IMPLIED DESIGN LOGIC
The following are reasonable but must remain visually modest and, if labelled, should read as `implied` or `reconstructed`:
- work surfaces suitable for crucibles and heated pans
- a controlled instructional layout rather than random workshop clutter
- modest materials storage
- one small staff or instructor support zone
- practical separation of circulation from hotter work areas
- some form of environmental robustness suitable for repeated sympathy use

Do not let inferred systems dominate the drawing.

## WHAT MUST NOT BE CONTRADICTED
Absolutely do not contradict:
- the presence of crucibles
- the presence of heated iron work
- the presence of water canister logic
- the sympathy teaching / lab context
- the building’s academic use
- the need for specialized work space 

## HARD BANS
Do not include:
- towers
- wings added for visual drama
- giant ceremonial halls
- cathedral vaulting for spectacle
- fantasy chimneys
- glowing rune floors
- grand mystical apparatus not supported by evidence
- residential quarters
- treasury logic
- prison logic
- heroic exterior stairs
- unnecessary courtyards
- extra bridges
- huge annexes
- overly complex underground systems
- giant foundry-scale industrial space unless clearly marked reconstructed and kept modest

## UNCERTAINTY HANDLING
If a detail is unknown:
- keep it generic
- keep it modest
- keep it functional
- label it `reconstructed` where appropriate

If the exact building size is unknown:
- keep the footprint conservative
- avoid over-expansion
- favor a compact academic work building or suite over a sprawling complex

If a new detail would force contradiction:
- omit it
- simplify it
- do not improvise

## GRAPHIC CONVENTIONS
Use:
- thick wall line for the main Crucible shell or bounded suite
- lighter lines for secondary partitions
- clear door swings where useful
- work stations only if architecturally relevant
- minimal furniture
- simple labels
- plain key:
  - `canon`
  - `implied`
  - `reconstructed`

Suggested labels:
- `entry`
- `instruction`
- `work area`
- `crucibles`
- `heated pans`
- `water`
- `materials`
- `storage`
- `service`

Do not over-label speculative details.

## SCOPE CONTROL
Show only:
- the Crucible
- its immediate exterior context
- the work and instruction zones needed to explain it
- minimal supporting space

Do not show:
- the whole University
- the full campus map
- unrelated nearby buildings in detail
- decorative settlement context
- any narrative scene

## FINAL IMAGE CHARACTER
The final image should feel like:
- a practical sympathy teaching and work facility
- old university infrastructure
- technically useful
- somewhat austere
- believable as part of the University
- restrained enough to support later refinement

It should not feel like:
- a fantasy wizard lab
- an alchemical cathedral
- a noble private laboratory
- an artificery factory unless overruled by later frozen evidence
- an invented magical spectacle

## FINAL QUALITY CHECK
Before finalizing, verify:
- the Crucible reads as a sympathy work site
- crucibles are clearly present
- heated-work logic is clearly present
- water support is clearly present
- the plan remains modest and academic
- the circulation is clear
- the plan does not invent excess rooms
- uncertainty is preserved conservatively
- the image can serve as a future reference drawing, not just a visual mood piece

- it is industrial in a restrained medieval-university way, not mystical or theatrical

## BUILDING / COMPLEX CHARACTER
The Fishery / Artificery should feel:
- academic
- practical
- industrial
- supervised
- hazardous in a controlled way
- workshop-like rather than residential
- more technical than ceremonial
- part of a real university rather than a wizard’s foundry

It should **not** feel:
- like a palace workshop
- like a temple
- like a giant foundry unless clearly marked reconstructed and kept modest
- like a dormitory
- like a fortress
- like a dungeon
- like a fantasy boss-room
- like a decorative artisan guildhall

## EXTERIOR CHARACTER
If shown as a distinct building, the exterior should read as:
- plain
- institutional
- workshop-adapted
- stone or stone-dominant if reconstruction requires a shell
- functional
- believable beside other University buildings
- not decorative
- not towered
- not chapel-like
- not militarized

The exterior should support:
- east-wall low-window logic
- practical yard access
- workshop loading / movement logic
- controlled circulation between interior and exterior work zones

Do **not** over-design the exterior silhouette.

## WORKSHOP LOGIC
Use only conservative workshop logic.

### Canon / near-canon zones
- `main workshop`
- `Kilvin's office`
- `south exit`
- `courtyard`
- `east wall low windows`
- `artificery work`
- `shop quarters`

### Strongly implied support zones
If needed, include only a small number of these:
- `materials`
- `storage`
- `benches`
- `repair`
- `service`
- `yard`
- `loading`
- `fuel / heat support`
- `student work`
- `instructor support`

### Labeling rule
Prefer generic functional labels over overconfident lore labels.

Good:
- `main workshop`
- `office`
- `yard`
- `storage`
- `materials`
- `service`
- `student work`
- `repair`
- `courtyard`

Avoid:
- `grand artificery hall`
- `arcane forge sanctum`
- `master engine room`
- `thaumic reactor`
- `legendary item chamber`
- any ornate or modernized invented terminology

## MAIN WORKSHOP REQUIREMENTS
The principal workshop zone should read as:
- the heart of the building
- a place for repeated practical work
- large enough for multiple users
- organized around work surfaces rather than ceremonial emptiness
- dangerous enough to need discipline
- not cluttered beyond legibility
- clearly specialized

It may include, if useful and restrained:
- long worktables or benches
- clear work bays
- material handling zones
- robust floors
- modest storage at edges
- separation between hot work and circulation

Do **not** over-furnish it.

## KILVIN’S OFFICE REQUIREMENTS
Kilvin’s office must read as:
- a real office or enclosed master’s room
- inside the workshop building or directly adjacent to it
- important, but secondary to the main workshop floor
- practical, not ceremonial
- supervisory rather than residential

It may include:
- one desk or table if architecturally useful
- modest storage
- direct or near-direct relation to the workshop

Do **not** turn it into a suite of grand chambers.

## EAST WALL / SOUTH EXIT REQUIREMENTS
These are especially important.

The plan must support:
- **low windows on the east wall**
- a **south exit** toward a courtyard

This means the layout should make it easy to understand:
- which side is east
- which edge is south
- how the courtyard or working-yard relation functions
- how exterior circulation works at the south side

Do **not** bury or confuse these two orientation anchors.

## COURTYARD / WORKING-YARD REQUIREMENTS
Because the south exit relates to a courtyard, the plan must show enough exterior context to explain that relationship.

Show only what is needed:
- a courtyard edge or working yard
- the south exit
- immediate circulation around that exit
- enough surrounding open ground or paved yard to make the relation clear

Do **not**:
- generate a full campus
- invent ornamental landscaping
- create a palace forecourt
- add unnecessary neighboring buildings
- turn the courtyard into a cloister
- make the yard feel ceremonial rather than practical

The courtyard / yard should feel:
- useful
- work-oriented
- plain
- believable for movement of people and materials

## DAMAGE / ROBUSTNESS LOGIC
Because the shop quarters can be damaged, the building should support:
- a robust workshop shell
- separable work bays or quarters if conservatively useful
- practical repairable architecture
- a sense that explosions, accidents, or workshop failure are a risk in the life of the building

But do **not** overbuild this into:
- blast walls
- modern safety bunkers
- fantasy containment vaults
- giant chimneys
- huge isolated furnaces unless clearly reconstructed and kept modest

## STRONGLY IMPLIED DESIGN LOGIC
The following are reasonable but must remain visually modest and, if labelled, should read as `implied` or `reconstructed`:
- multiple work areas within the main workshop
- material storage
- some separation between student work and more controlled master-level work
- practical exterior service access
- one or more internal support zones
- durable construction suitable for hazardous craft

Do **not** let these implied elements dominate the drawing.

## WHAT MUST NOT BE CONTRADICTED
Absolutely do not contradict:
- the presence of workshop space
- Kilvin’s office being inside or adjacent
- the practical industrial identity of the building
- the south exit toward a courtyard
- the low east-wall windows
- the non-residential character
- the Fishery / Artificery relation
- the building’s role in sympathy / artificery practical work

## HARD BANS
Do not include:
- towers
- giant ceremonial halls
- decorative furnaces
- fantasy smokestacks
- glowing rune floors
- heroic exterior stairs
- residential quarters
- chapel logic
- guildhall ceremony spaces
- unnecessary bridges
- ornamental courtyards
- excessive annexes
- over-complex underground systems
- a full-campus map
- a giant foundry-scale industrial city unless clearly marked reconstructed and kept restrained

## UNCERTAINTY HANDLING
If a detail is unknown:
- keep it generic
- keep it modest
- keep it functional
- label it `reconstructed` where appropriate

If the exact building size is unknown:
- keep the footprint conservative
- avoid over-expansion
- favor a believable academic workshop block over a sprawling industrial fantasy

If the Fishery / Artificery relationship is uncertain:
- keep them visually linked but not overdefined
- avoid pretending a precise separation or merger is canon unless frozen elsewhere

If a new detail would force contradiction:
- omit it
- simplify it
- do not improvise

## GRAPHIC CONVENTIONS
Use:
- thick wall line for the main workshop shell or bounded complex
- lighter lines for secondary partitions and reconstructed yard edges
- clear door swings where useful
- work stations only if architecturally relevant
- minimal furniture
- simple labels
- plain key:
  - `canon`
  - `implied`
  - `reconstructed`

Suggested labels:
- `main workshop`
- `Kilvin's office`
- `south exit`
- `courtyard`
- `yard`
- `storage`
- `materials`
- `service`
- `student work`
- `repair`

Do **not** over-label speculative details.

## SCOPE CONTROL
Show only:
- the Fishery / Artificery
- its immediate exterior context
- the workshop and office zones needed to explain it
- the courtyard / yard relation at the south side
- minimal supporting space

Do **not** show:
- the whole University
- the full campus plan
- unrelated nearby buildings in detail
- decorative settlement context
- narrative scene elements

## FINAL IMAGE CHARACTER
The final image should feel like:
- a practical university workshop complex
- old institutional craft infrastructure
- technically useful
- somewhat austere
- believable as part of the University
- restrained enough to support later refinement

It should not feel like:
- a fantasy forge-palace
- an alchemical cathedral
- a decorative artisan compound
- a fully industrial modern plant
- an invented magical spectacle

## FINAL QUALITY CHECK
Before finalizing, verify:
- the building reads as a workshop complex
- Kilvin’s office is clearly inside or adjacent
- the south exit toward a courtyard is clear
- the east wall supports low-window logic
- the plan remains practical and academic-industrial
- the circulation is clear
- the plan does not invent excess rooms
- uncertainty is preserved conservatively
- the image can serve as a future reference drawing, not just a mood image
