# The Archives — AI Image Generation Prompt Pack

_Canon-constrained visual identity sheet for The Archives (Kingkiller Chronicle).  
Intended for use AFTER the master campus site plan and the Archives' approved floor plan are frozen. This pack is the building-wide identity sheet, not the authoritative floor-by-floor prompt._

Sources: _The Name of the Wind_ (NOTW) and _The Wise Man's Fear_ (WMF) by Patrick Rothfuss. Inline chapter citations given for every locked claim.

Labelling convention used throughout this pack:

- **canon** — stated or unambiguously shown in the text.
- **implied** — necessarily true given what the text shows (e.g. ventilation must exist because the air is dry and fresh in a windowless building).
- **reconstructed** — plausible fill chosen by the reconstructor; should be called out and never rendered as if it were canon.

---

## Base Style Guidelines

- Medieval academic stone architecture, late-medieval scholarly / early-institutional register.
- Severe, monolithic exterior; no carved ornament, no corbels, no gargoyles, no heraldry.
- Interior light is entirely artificial — sympathy lamps producing a distinctive unwavering reddish light _(NOTW Ch 36)_. Never sunlight, never gaslight, never modern fluorescent or warm-white electric.
- Technical clarity over fantasy flourish.
- Dominant sensory register: dust, dryness, silence, leather, parchment, cool stone _(NOTW Ch 43)_.
- Institutional hierarchy is legible in the architecture itself: public antechamber, semi-public reading room, restricted stacks, restricted service routes, sealed deep levels.

---

## Locked Canon (hard constraints)

### Exterior
1. Six-storey square grey stone monolith; resembles an immense greystone (i.e. a crude, unadorned rectangular block of dressed grey stone) _(NOTW Ch 36)_.
2. No windows anywhere on any elevation _(NOTW Ch 36)_.
3. No exterior decoration or ornament of any kind _(NOTW Ch 36)_.
4. ONE exterior public entrance only: a massive pair of stone doors on a single face, wide enough and heavy enough to stand permanently open during operating hours _(NOTW Ch 36)_.
5. The doorway is framed by a deep-chiselled inscription on the stone lintel above the doors: **Vorfelan Rhinata Morie**. Language is not Siaru; possibly Yllish or Temic; untranslated within the text _(NOTW Ch 36)_.
6. The building extends below ground — stairwells in the Stacks descend into sub-levels. At least one named lower level: Sub-three (reached by two separate long flights of stone steps from an upper stacks floor, then a long wide low-ceilinged hallway) _(NOTW Ch 43; WMF, Puppet introduction)_.
7. The building visibly rises above the surrounding tree line: visible from the crest of Stonebridge, to the west, rising like a great greystone over the trees _(NOTW Ch 36)_.
8. The Archives sits adjacent to the Medica — the Medica is on the far side of the Archives from one of the main courtyards _(NOTW Ch 41)_.

### Threshold sequence (canon, in order)
1. Approach reads as a blank grey wall with a single monumental stone portal.
2. Through the stone doors: a small antechamber. Bare grey stone walls. Lit by sympathy lamps with unwavering reddish light. A large wooden desk with several open ledger books; a scriv stationed at it _(NOTW Ch 36)_.
3. From the antechamber, three onward routes branch:
   - A double set of wooden doors labelled **TOMES** — public reading room.
   - A double set of wooden doors labelled **STACKS** — Arcanum-only shelving.
   - A smaller single door, sited behind the desk, labelled **SCRIVS ONLY** — service route for scrivs to fetch requested books _(NOTW Ch 36)_.

### Tomes (public reading room)
- Large, high-ceilinged rectangular room. Long tables; dozens of sympathy lamps giving the room unwavering reddish light _(NOTW Ch 38)_.
- Shelving along one wall holding three to four hundred reference tomes used for the basic classes. The rest of the collection is held in the Stacks and fetched by scrivs on request _(NOTW Ch 38)_.
- Unnaturally quiet; nicknamed "Tombs" by students for the cryptlike silence _(NOTW Ch 38)_.
- Request workflow: readers write titles or topics in a log book at a scriv's desk inside Tomes; the scriv disappears through the service route into the Stacks and returns with the book. Typical wait up to an hour _(NOTW Ch 38)_.

### Stacks (Arcanum-only)
- Vast, multi-level shelving maze. Six storeys tall above grade plus the underground levels _(NOTW Ch 43)_.
- **Deliberately unlit.** Permanent lighting would damage the books, and the building is too large to illuminate. Readers must carry single hand lamps — purchased for 1½ talents or signed out free at the antechamber desk. Open flame is forbidden anywhere inside _(NOTW Ch 43)_.
- Air is cool, dry, still; smells of old leather, parchment, and forgotten secrets _(NOTW Ch 43)_.
- Stairwells descend as well as ascend; at least one is the south-east stairwell _(NOTW Ch 43)_.
- Interior contains:
  - Small stone talk-rooms with heavy wooden doors and a table — for small groups to meet without disturbing the silence _(NOTW Ch 43)_.
  - Reading holes — small featureless private rooms with a table and two chairs, reached from behind the antechamber scriv's desk down a flight of stairs, scattered throughout the building. Spot-checked periodically by scrivs to ensure nobody sleeps or has sex in them _(NOTW Ch 38, Ch 90)_.
- Holdings: over three-quarters of a million volumes, plus clay tablets, scrolls, and the Caluptena fragments _(NOTW Ch 90)_.
- The only section of interior stone wall not lined with books is the wall holding the four-plate door (see below) _(NOTW Ch 90)_.

### Four-plate door (Valaritas) — deep Stacks, lower level
- Reached by descending several flights of stairs through a maze of corridors and shelves _(NOTW Ch 90)_.
- Set in the only stretch of stone wall in the building without shelving _(NOTW Ch 90)_.
- Door is a single piece of grey stone; frame is a single piece of grey stone, 8 inches wide; all flush — door flush with frame, frame flush with wall. No hinges, no handle, no window, no sliding panel _(NOTW Ch 43)_.
- Four copper plates inset flush with the face of the door; each has a non-conventional keyhole _(NOTW Ch 43)_.
- Between the four copper plates, the word **VALARITAS** is chiselled deep into the stone _(NOTW Ch 43)_.
- The door is not for opening. It is a door for staying closed _(NOTW Ch 43)_.

### Service back-door from the Underthing (lowest level)
- The Archives' ventilation system discharges through the Underthing sub-area called Billows, at 100+ ft below ground _(NOTW Ch 90)_.
- A locked door exists at the lowest level of the Stacks, opening onto a low (≈3 ft clearance) crawl-passage into Billows. Its purpose is scriv access to the ventilation system _(NOTW Ch 90)_.
- This is the building's ONLY non-public access point. It must not be rendered as an obvious exterior feature — from outside, it is invisible, since it enters via the subterranean network, not through the building's outer walls.

### Sub-three and Puppet's chambers (deep Stacks, WMF)
- Sub-three: reached from the upper Stacks by a long flight of stone steps (worn concave by centuries of use, looking like an abandoned riverbed in lamplight), then a second flight, then a long stretch of wide low-ceilinged hallway _(WMF, Puppet introduction)_.
- Puppet's door at the end of Sub-three is unremarkable — would be mistaken for one of the countless reading holes scattered through the Stacks _(WMF)_.
- Puppet's chambers themselves contain candlelight (explicitly forbidden elsewhere in the Archives) — Lorren does not know _(WMF)_. **This is a canon anomaly, not an invitation to render candles generally**.

### Roof
- Bare stone rooftop, accessible only via a single internal wooden door that is normally kept locked. Lorren holds the sole key _(WMF, Archives storm incident)_.
- No second key exists. When Elodin and Kvothe were trapped on the roof, scrivs had to batter the door down from the inside to reach them _(WMF)_.
- No parapet ornamentation described. If a parapet is shown at all, keep it minimal, undressed, and institutional.

---

## Hard Bans

Do not render any of the following, regardless of any prompt-chaining that suggests otherwise:

- No windows on any elevation.
- No second, third, or "hidden" public entrance. Only the single stone doorway, and the invisible subterranean scriv route.
- No decorative towers, turrets, spires, or campanile. The silhouette is a single blank cube.
- No carved heraldry, statuary, friezes, gargoyles, relief panels, or pilasters.
- No stained glass. (That belongs to Hollows.)
- No bright, modern, warm-white, electric, gas, or fluorescent lighting. All interior light is sympathy-lamp red, or candle-flame in Puppet's chambers only.
- No neat single-room "great hall" interpretation of the building. The interior must read as a stacked multi-storey maze, not as one cathedral-like space.
- No forecourt, palace plaza, grand staircase approach, or ceremonial axial sight-line. The entrance is severe and near-hostile.
- No contemporary bookshop clichés (ladders on rails, green banker's lamps, reading nooks with armchairs). Tomes has long tables; the Stacks have shelves and hand-lamp darkness.

---

## Building Overview Prompt (for image-gen tools)

> Generate a canon-constrained visual overview of The Archives from Patrick Rothfuss's _Kingkiller Chronicle_.
>
> **Locked canon (do not contradict):**
> - Six-storey square grey stone monolith; block-like; silhouette of an oversized greystone.
> - No windows, no exterior ornament, no towers, no heraldry.
> - One exterior entrance only: a massive pair of stone doors on a single face, wide enough and heavy enough to stand permanently open during the day.
> - A deep-chiselled inscription above the doors reads **Vorfelan Rhinata Morie**.
> - Inside: a small antechamber of bare grey stone lit by sympathy lamps with unwavering reddish light, containing a wooden scriv's desk with ledger books.
> - From the antechamber, three onward routes: **TOMES** (public reading), **STACKS** (Arcanum-only), and a smaller **SCRIVS ONLY** service door behind the desk.
> - Tomes is a high-ceilinged rectangular reading room, lit by dozens of sympathy lamps, long tables, one wall of reference shelving (three to four hundred books), otherwise bare stone.
> - The Stacks are deliberately unlit — navigated only by single hand-lamps; cool, dry air; smell of old leather and parchment; six storeys above ground plus multiple sub-levels reached by stairwells; shelving runs so densely that the only section of interior stone wall without shelves is the wall holding the four-plate door.
> - Deep in the Stacks, on a lower level, a single piece of grey stone flush-set in a grey stone frame, with four flush-set copper plates each holding a non-conventional keyhole, and the word **VALARITAS** chiselled deep between them.
> - Sub-three and Puppet's chambers at the far end of a long, wide, low-ceilinged hallway after two long flights of worn stone steps; Puppet's candle-lit room is an anomaly that should read as a secret, not an exception to the no-fire rule.
> - A locked scriv service door at the lowest level of the Stacks connects to the subterranean Underthing (Billows ventilation tunnels) via a 3-ft-clearance crawl-passage.
> - The roof is bare stone, reached only by a single locked internal wooden door (key held by the Master Archivist).
>
> **Style register:**
> - Medieval academic stone architecture; severe, monolithic, institutional.
> - Interior light is entirely sympathy-lamp red, unwavering, not warm; never modern lighting, never daylight.
> - Dust, dryness, silence, and hierarchy are the dominant sensory notes.
> - Technical clarity over fantasy flourish.
>
> **Hard bans:** no windows; no second entrance; no decorative towers; no stained glass; no bright modern library lighting; no single-room great-hall interpretation; no palace forecourt; no ornamental facade elements of any kind.

---

## Scene Set

Each scene below is a separate render brief. Use these in sequence to build the pack.

### Scene 1 — Exterior (approach)
- Single face of the six-storey grey stone cube.
- Monumental stone doorway, doors standing open; inscription _Vorfelan Rhinata Morie_ chiselled into the lintel.
- No windows visible on any elevation in sight.
- Surrounding context: a restrained, institutional campus setting, low-rise and tree-dotted; Medica implied to one side (do not render it in detail unless the adjacent building brief calls for it).
- Mood: unadorned, cool, heavy, silent.

### Scene 2 — Threshold (antechamber)
- Compact rectangular antechamber of bare grey stone.
- Sympathy lamps give unwavering reddish-amber light; shadows are soft and static, not flickering.
- Large wooden desk with open ledger books; a single scriv present.
- Three labelled doors visible in the back wall: **TOMES** (left, double wooden doors), **STACKS** (right, double wooden doors), **SCRIVS ONLY** (smaller single door behind the desk). Labels are plain cut or painted lettering, not ornamental signage.

### Scene 3 — Tomes (public reading room)
- High-ceilinged rectangular hall. Stone walls, timber-beamed ceiling.
- Dozens of sympathy lamps distributed so the room is fully and evenly lit in unwavering reddish light.
- Long tables, wooden chairs, students reading.
- One wall lined with shelving holding three to four hundred reference volumes.
- Otherwise bare. No decoration. Dead silence is implied by posture and absence of gesture.

### Scene 4 — Stacks (Arcanum shelving maze)
- Dense, towering shelving running in long corridors, stacked floor-to-ceiling.
- Total darkness except for a single hand-lamp's reddish bubble of light, held by one figure. Rest of the scene drops to black within a few metres.
- Air reads as dry and still; loose leather bindings, parchment labels, old paper.
- Occasional glimpses of stairwells descending or rising, and small heavy wooden doors to stone talk-rooms or reading holes off to one side.
- Scale must read vertical: the ceiling is not seen, only implied.

### Scene 5 — Deep Stacks / Sub-three
- Lower level reached by worn concave stone steps (described as looking like an abandoned riverbed in lamplight).
- Long wide corridor with a low ceiling; shelves on both sides.
- At the end, an unremarkable plain wooden door — readable as one of the many reading holes, not as a special chamber. This is Puppet's door.
- Behind Puppet's door (separate render if required): warm candlelight only, in contrast to the sympathy-lamp red everywhere else in the building. Cluttered with hanging puppets, fabric, a sense of inhabitation. This anomaly must feel hidden, not officially sanctioned.

### Scene 6 — Four-plate door (Valaritas)
- A section of bare grey stone wall, in a corner of the lower Stacks, where the shelving stops and the wall is blank.
- A rectangular door-shape, flush-set in a grey stone frame 8 inches wide — door and frame both single pieces of grey stone, all flush with the surrounding wall. A single pass of the hand across the surface could barely feel the seams.
- Four copper plates set flush into the face of the door, each with a single non-conventional keyhole.
- Between the plates, chiselled deep into the stone, the word **VALARITAS**.
- Mood: a door for staying closed. Not decorative. Absolutely still.

### Scene 7 — Service Tunnel entry (Underthing-side, reconstructed)
- Subterranean stone tunnel (Billows, Underthing network) with a steady rushing wind — dust and leather in the air.
- An ancient, moldering wooden door set into a rough stone wall; a low 3-ft-clearance crawl-passage beyond.
- No exterior-of-Archives view in this scene. This entry is invisible from above ground.
- Render cues: cold, damp-edged, utilitarian, locked.

### Scene 8 — Roof
- Bare, featureless stone rooftop; no parapet detail unless conservative.
- A single weathered wooden door set flush in a small stone stairhead housing. The door is clearly meant to be locked from the inside; no mechanism visible on the roof side.
- Sky overhead; no towers, no chimneys, no ornaments, no skylights. The roof is a bare stone expanse.

## Site Plan Prompt (companion)

> Create a top-down architectural site plan of The Archives and its immediate exterior context from the _Kingkiller Chronicle_ as a canon-constrained reconstruction, not a fantasy invention.
>
> **Style:**
> - Clean black-and-white planning drawing.
> - North arrow.
> - Simple labels.
> - No decorative fantasy art; no characters; no dramatic lighting.
> - Measured, restrained, scholarly presentation.
>
> **Locked canon shell:**
> - Square footprint; six storeys above grade; extends below grade into at least Sub-three and deeper.
> - Blank grey stone elevations on all four faces.
> - Single public entrance: a monumental stone doorway on ONE face. The entrance face is canon-underdetermined — pick one, label it "reconstructed: entrance face," and keep it consistent across all downstream drawings.
> - Inscription _Vorfelan Rhinata Morie_ on the lintel over the public doors.
> - Immediate adjacency: the Medica sits on the far side of the Archives from one of the main courtyards — label the Medica as **canon-adjacent**, do not draw its interior. Stonebridge is over two miles east of the University across the Omethi — do not draw Stonebridge here.
>
> **Interior facts that should influence (but not break) the site plan:**
> - Two-stage threshold (stone doors → antechamber → three internal doors) on the single entrance face.
> - Public-vs-restricted split is internal, not expressed externally.
> - A subterranean service connection to the Underthing (Billows) exists at the building's lowest level — show as a dashed, subterranean tunnel stub outside the south-east corner if a stair position is needed; mark as **canon, location reconstructed**.
> - Deep named levels (Sub-three, and deeper) exist but MUST NOT protrude above grade or force exterior annexes.
>
> **Labelling key (mandatory):**
> - canon — stated in text.
> - implied — necessarily true given the text.
> - reconstructed — reconstructor's fill; flag clearly.
>
> **Hard bans:**
> - No windows.
> - No decorative facade treatment.
> - No multiple public doors.
> - No attached fantasy towers, spires, or annexes.
> - No forecourt, plaza, garden, or palace approach.
> - No roof ornament.
>
> **Rules:**
> - Show only the Archives and the immediate exterior context needed to orient it.
> - If any single detail is uncertain, label it "reconstructed" rather than implying canon.
> - If a new detail would force a contradiction with earlier approved plans, REJECT the detail rather than improvise.

---

## Consistency Requirements (applies to every scene and plan in this pack)

- North arrow must align with the master campus map. Do not rotate between drawings.
- Scale must stay consistent with the approved building shell — a six-storey cube of fixed footprint.
- All previously approved entrances, corridors, stairs, and adjacent rooms must be preserved unchanged.
- No drawing in this pack may contradict an earlier approved drawing in this pack.
- If a new detail would force a contradiction, reject the detail. Do not improvise a resolution; return to the reconstruction brief for a canon check.
- All reconstructed elements must be labelled "reconstructed" visibly in any plan or key. They must never be rendered as if they were canon.

  -> interior (NOTW Ch 36).

  ledger books; a scriv is stationed at it. The room is lit entirely
  by sympathy lamps giving unwavering reddish light.

  - `TOMES` — double wooden doors to the central public reading hall.
  - `STACKS` — double wooden doors to Arcanum-only shelving access.
  - `SCRIVS ONLY` — a smaller single door, sited behind the counter

  tables for readers; one wall lined with reference tomes (three to
  four hundred volumes). Lit by dozens of sympathy lamps (not
  daylight, not windows). Unnaturally quiet — nicknamed `Tombs`
  (NOTW Ch 38).
- The `Stacks` access door on the ground floor opens onto restricted
  circulation that begins the Arcanum-only maze; stairs both descend
  to the lower Stacks and rise into the floors above.

Reconstructed programme (flag clearly; do not render as canon):
- West wing, off the `SCRIVS ONLY` door: Scriv's Office, Transcription
  Room, Copying Room, Scriv's Rest.
- East wing, off the `STACKS` door: Catalogue Room, Lamp Larder,
  Unpacking Room, Restricted Shelving (initial entry), Vertical
  Transport Shaft.
- Stairs: at least one stair down to the lower Stacks, at least one
  stair up into the floors above.
- An internal lantern-roof detail may be drawn over `Tomes` ONLY as an
  interior clerestory / raised ceiling feature. It is NOT a skylight,
  NOT a window, NOT an opening to sky.

  — those belong in the deep Stacks

- no people

Thought for 2m 7s
I tightened it around the strongest book details: the approach is by road north through forest to a path over a vast lawn, toward a huge manor-house complex bigger than the Artificery, with a red tile roof, high windows, arched doorways, pillars, and an unclimbable wrought-iron fence around the grounds. Here’s a more Rothfuss-accurate version:

Create a **strictly top-down, orthographic architectural floor plan** of **Haven, Ground Floor** from Patrick Rothfuss’s *Kingkiller Chronicle* as a **canon-constrained reconstruction**, not a fantasy invention.

This is **not** a full campus map.
This is **not** a decorative fantasy illustration.
This is **not** a dungeon diagram or a cinematic asylum concept painting.

The image must function as a **technical planning reference** for Haven’s ground floor:
- the formal arrival sequence
- the impressive public / intake front
- the main circulation logic
- the first layer of ward organization
- the difference between ordinary care space and more controlled space
- the sense of a very large institutional manor-asylum rather than a tiny infirmary or prison maze

## CORE INTENT
Reconstruct Haven’s ground floor as faithfully as possible to the books while staying restrained, practical, and believable as part of the University.

The drawing must:
- prioritize direct book evidence first
- preserve uncertainty where evidence stops
- avoid over-inventing named rooms
- avoid turning Haven into a gothic fantasy madhouse
- keep the front of the building formal and impressive
- keep the deeper circulation more controlled and institutional
- preserve the distinction between ordinary wards and specialized containment

## DRAWING MODE
Use:
- true top-down orthographic view
- black-and-white architectural floor plan
- clear walls, doors, windows where canon supports them, corridors, stairs, and room boundaries
- simple labels
- north arrow
- scale bar
- plain legend / key
- certainty categories:
  - `canon`
  - `implied`
  - `reconstructed`

Do **not** use:
- perspective
- isometric view
- dramatic shadows
- decorative fantasy art
- chains, cages, torture imagery, or theatrical horror
- heraldry
- characters
- parchment textures
- theatrical lighting
- invented grandeur beyond what the books already support

## PRIMARY RECONSTRUCTION TARGET
The target is **Haven as the University asylum**, also called the **Rookery** and the **Crockery**, with a formal public ground floor and more controlled ward circulation beyond it.

The drawing must therefore show:
1. enough exterior edge to explain the principal entry
2. the formal entry hall / intake space
3. the entrance desk / ledger logic
4. the broad stair rising from the public front
5. one or more long white ward corridors lined with wooden doors
6. a believable split between more ordinary patient accommodation and more supervised / secure areas
7. only the minimum additional room logic needed to make the plan useful

## LOCKED CANON FACTS
Represent these as the strongest anchors:

- Haven is approached from the north by road / path, through grounds and gate, toward a huge manor-like building.
- The building is **bigger than the Artificery**.
- Exterior character includes: **red tile roof, high windows, arched doorways, pillars, fountains, flowers, hedges**, and a **wrought-iron fence nearly ten feet tall and unclimbable**.
- The front doors are substantial enough that a doorman unlocks and opens them.
- The **entryway is huge**, with **stained glass windows**, **vaulted ceilings**, and a **marble floor polished to a mirror sheen**.
- A **large desk** stands in the entry area with ledger logic.
- A **wide marble staircase** rises from that front zone.
- Beyond that are **long white hallways lined with wooden doors**.
- Haven is very large: roughly **320–350** current patients with room for **another 150 easy**.
- Whin is explicitly placed on the **second floor east, room 247**, so his padded room is not automatically a ground-floor canon room.
- Specialized rooms exist elsewhere in Haven, including at least Whin’s padded room and Elodin’s old copper-doored confinement room, but those should not be forced onto the ground floor without clear segregation and `reconstructed` labeling. 

## SCALE AND CHARACTER
The ground floor must feel:
- institutional
- large
- expensive by University standards
- eerily quiet
- formal at the front
- controlled deeper in
- more manor-asylum than prison
- more polished than the rest of the University’s rougher work buildings

It must **not** feel:
- tiny
- like a village clinic
- like a faculty office block
- like a dungeon labyrinth
- like a government palace
- like a fortress barracks
- like a decorative noble estate without medical / custodial function

## EXTERIOR / ENTRY EDGE
Show only enough exterior context to orient the entry:
- edge of approach path or forecourt
- front doors
- some hint of formal grounds if needed
- but keep exterior detail minimal and secondary to the floor plan

Do **not** attempt a full site plan of all Haven grounds here.

## FRONT-OF-HOUSE / PUBLIC INTAKE ZONE
This front zone should include:
- principal entrance
- huge entry hall
- stained-glass / vaulted-volume logic
- polished marble floor character
- large desk / reception / ledger area
- broad stair rising upward
- immediate controlled transition into the institution beyond

This zone should read as:
- formal
- impressive
- supervised
- quieter and more polished than the ward corridors
- a place where new arrivals, staff, and visitors would be received and directed

Do **not** make it:
- a ceremonial throne hall
- a chapel nave
- a ballroom
- a fantasy atrium

## MAIN CIRCULATION LOGIC
The books strongly support:
- entry hall
- desk
- broad stair
- long white corridors with wooden doors

So the floor plan should clearly communicate:
- a strong front-to-back movement sequence
- a central or near-central entry block
- one or more corridors extending into ward space
- increasing control as one goes deeper
- a clearer hierarchy than in Mains or the Archives

Circulation should feel:
- legible
- institutional
- supervised
- somewhat formal at first
- progressively more controlled deeper in

## WARD AND ROOM LOGIC
Use conservative room logic.

### Canon / near-canon front-level elements
- `entry hall`
- `desk`
- `broad stair`
- `corridor`
- `ward`
- `supervision`
- `service`

### Strongly implied room types
If needed, include only a few of these:
- `intake`
- `reception`
- `staff`
- `ward room`
- `safer room`
- `secure corridor`
- `service room`
- `linen / stores`
- `observation`
- `attendant room`

### Labeling rule
Prefer simple generic labels over lore-heavy invention.

Good:
- `entry hall`
- `desk`
- `ward`
- `supervision`
- `service`
- `secure corridor`
- `safer room`
- `stores`

Avoid:
- `mad wing`
- `insanity vault`
- `wailing hall`
- `Master Namer's prison`
- any theatrical or sensational label

## SECURITY / ROOM-TYPE HIERARCHY
The plan should support at least three different levels of room logic:

1. **Ordinary ward logic**
   - repetitive patient rooms or ward rooms
   - less restrictive
   - still institutional

2. **Safer supervised logic**
   - more observation
   - more controlled access
   - fewer direct exits
   - suitable for patients who need closer oversight

3. **Extreme containment logic**
   - proven to exist somewhere in Haven
   - should be **kept reconstructed and segregated** if shown on this floor
   - should not dominate the ground floor unless you clearly mark it as a conservative reconstruction branch

The ground floor should not imply that every room is equally dangerous or equally secure.

## WHAT TO DO WITH WHIN AND ELODIN EVIDENCE
Use these carefully:

- **Whin’s room** proves that specialized padded accommodation exists, but because Whin is explicitly on the **second floor east**, do not place his named room on the ground floor as canon.
- **Elodin’s old copper-doored room** proves extreme containment exists somewhere in Haven, but do not force that exact room onto the ground floor unless shown as a highly uncertain reconstructed secure branch.

If you include either logic on the ground floor:
- segregate it
- label it `reconstructed`
- keep it secondary to the main manor-institution front

## DESIGN BRIDGE
Use this as the main reconstruction bridge:

A **large formal entry block** with a **reception / ledger desk** and **broad stair** feeds into **one or more long white ward corridors**, with at least one **more controlled side branch or secure wing** that remains modest and clearly marked if uncertain.

This bridge is plausible, canon-friendly, and should govern the composition.

## WINDOWS
Unlike the Archives, Haven **does have windows**.
The books support:
- high exterior windows
- stained-glass windows in the entryway

So windows are allowed where justified, especially in:
- the entry hall
- exterior ward walls
- formal front rooms

Do **not** overdo them into decorative cathedral glazing.
Keep them functional and institutionally elegant.

## HARD BANS
Do not include:
- Masters’ offices
- faculty governance chambers
- classrooms
- tiny clinic scale
- prison-block monotony replacing the formal manor front
- dungeon maze logic replacing the corridor system
- fantasy torture spaces
- ornamental gothic horror features
- elaborate underground prison levels
- over-named lore rooms with no textual support

## UNCERTAINTY HANDLING
If a detail is unknown:
- keep it generic
- keep it modest
- keep it functional
- label it `reconstructed` where appropriate

If a secure wing is uncertain:
- mark it `reconstructed`
- keep it compact
- avoid overpopulating it with named special rooms

If a new detail would force contradiction:
- omit it
- simplify it
- do not improvise

## GRAPHIC CONVENTIONS
Use:
- thick wall line for major outer walls
- lighter lines for internal partitions
- clear door swings where useful
- broad stair clearly shown
- windows only where justified
- simple labels
- plain key:
  - `canon`
  - `implied`
  - `reconstructed`

Suggested labels:
- `entry hall`
- `desk`
- `broad stair`
- `ward`
- `supervision`
- `service`
- `secure corridor`
- `safer room`
- `stores`

Do not over-label speculative details.

## SCOPE CONTROL
Show only:
- Haven ground floor
- minimal exterior edge needed to explain the entry
- the main arrival block
- the broad stair
- the ward corridors and differentiated care logic
- minimal support rooms

Do **not** show:
- the whole University
- the whole Haven grounds
- the second floor
- Whin’s exact room as ground-floor canon
- Elodin’s exact old room as ground-floor canon
- decorative landscape scenes

## FINAL IMAGE CHARACTER
The final image should feel like:
- a large University asylum with real funding behind it
- an institutional manor adapted for care and confinement
- eerily quiet and controlled
- formal at the front, more managed beyond
- technically useful as a floor-plan reference

It should not feel like:
- a fantasy madhouse
- a gothic dungeon
- a tiny medical cottage
- a palace state room
- a fully solved prison architecture diagram

## FINAL QUALITY CHECK
Before finalizing, verify:
- the entry hall is large and impressive
- stained-glass / vaulted / marble-front logic is preserved
- the desk is present in the front zone
- the broad stair is present
- long white corridor logic is clearly represented
- the building feels large enough for hundreds of patients
- room types are differentiated
- specialized containment remains segregated and cautious
- Whin is not wrongly treated as a ground-floor canon room
- the plan stays Rothfuss-accurate, not sensationalized

it now anchors the whole design around the actual front-of-house sequence from the book,

it keeps Whin off the ground floor,

it treats Elodin’s old cell as evidence of a secure wing, not as automatic ground-floor canon,

and it keeps Haven as a huge, expensive, eerily quiet manor-asylum, which is much closer to Rothfuss than “generic secure hospital.”

Create a top-down floor plan for Haven (also called the Rookery and the
Crockery), Ground Floor, from the Kingkiller Chronicle as a canon-
constrained reconstruction.

Canon (must not contradict):
- Haven is the University's asylum. Its nicknames: the Rookery
  ("because it's where you go if you're a-ravin'") and the Crockery
  (NOTW Ch 46). Its purpose is custodial containment of students and
  staff whose minds have broken — predominantly arcanists who have
  gone mad from naming and sympathy work, not from ciphering and
  grammar (NOTW Ch 46).
- Position: the northern edge of the University, separated from the
  academic core by a forested buffer. Approach is a wide dirt road
  that curves through forest so trees block the view of University
  buildings behind (NOTW Ch 46).
- Scale: "the size of the royal palace," bigger than the Artificery
  (NOTW Ch 46). Current inmates 320–350; capacity roughly 500. The
  ground floor must read institutional at that scale.
- Exterior register: late-medieval or early-modern GRAND MANOR HOUSE.
  Elegant lines, red tile roof, HIGH WINDOWS, arched doorways, pillars
  (NOTW Ch 46). NOT a fortress, castle, or dungeon exterior.
- Grounds: broad dressed lawns with fountains, flowers, hedges.
  Perimeter is a wrought-iron fence nearly ten feet tall, designed
  unclimbable; one formal main gate, staffed by two gatemen (NOTW
  Ch 46).
- Arrival sequence on the ground floor:
  - Formal approach path from the gate across the lawn.
  - THICK TIMBER front doors, locked from inside; a posted doorman
    (Jeremy in-text) unlocks on arrival.
  - A HUGE entryway with STAINED GLASS windows and VAULTED CEILINGS.
    Floor is MARBLE polished to a mirror sheen. Space is eerily
    silent — upper-floor sounds are muffled, implying thick floors
    (NOTW Ch 46).
  - A large wooden reception desk, staffed by a clerk (Emmie in-text),
    with LEDGER BOOKS shelved behind it. Inmates are tracked by wing
    and numbered room (e.g. "2nd floor east, 247").
  - A WIDE MARBLE STAIRCASE rises from the entryway to the upper
    floors. This is the canonical route up.
- Outdoor patient access is canonical: on calmer days inmates are let
  out into the grounds; on wild days (storms, full moon) they are
  kept in. This implies a supervised interior route onto the gardens
  on the ground floor (NOTW Ch 46).
- Building structure: walls throughout use "all-meshed stone" with
  wards on doors and windows — Haven is deliberately built to contain
  arcanists, not ordinary madmen (NOTW Ch 44, Simmon). This is true
  of the whole building; on this floor it should read in the wall
  thicknesses, not in visible copper or obvious wards.

NOT on this floor (flag as upper-floor material; reject if generator
tries to place them here):
- Whin's standard padded room — 2nd floor east, Room 247. Tall windows
  admitting daylight, thick white cloth padding on walls/ceiling/floor,
  heavy wooden door with sliding eye-level panel, lockable from
  outside.
- Leather-restraint rooms (wrist, waist, neck, ankle straps) off upper
  hallways.
- Elodin's former copper-door cell — DIFFERENT WING, upper floor,
  ~20 ft above the gardens with an unreachable balcony outside. Bare
  grey stone walls and ceiling, solid copper door, copper-threaded
  windows with reddish streaks, naming-damping air.

Reconstructed programme for the ground floor (flag clearly; do not
render as canon):
- Entrance hall / reception and grand stair (canon skeleton, interior
  layout reconstructed).
- Administrative suite behind reception: records office, keys and
  duty register, supervisor's station.
- Intake and assessment rooms (plausibly required at this scale).
- Staff day room, staff mess, rostered handler quarters.
- Kitchens, larders, laundry, and a service spine for a 500-bed
  institution (implied, not described).
- Calm-wing or low-security day rooms — rooms for the most stable
  patients who can be trusted near the entrance and garden — with
  supervised interior doors out onto the walled gardens. Flag this
  whole wing as reconstructed; canon does not place it.
- Secondary / service entrance for deliveries (food, laundry, coal),
  separate from the formal front doors.

Hard bans:
- no Masters' offices
- no faculty governance chamber
- no tiny clinic scale
- no dungeon maze replacing the formal manor interior
- no windowless shell — Haven has canonical high windows and stained
  glass
- no fortress, castle, or prison exterior
- no named inmate rooms on the ground floor (those are upper-floor
  canon)
- no copper cells, leather-restraint rooms, or padded wards on the
  ground floor
- no central open courtyard inside the building (the grounds wrap the
  exterior; the interior is manor-house)
- no modern hospital cues — no triage counters, nurse stations, crash
  carts, or wheelchairs

Drawing style:
architectural floor plan
black and white
clear walls, doors, stairs, corridors, windows (windows are
justified here — Haven is not the Archives)
room labels
north arrow
scale bar
no people
no decorative fantasy elements

- Keep the formal entry monumental and the deeper ground-floor

  - Grand public face (entryway, reception, grand stair).

   - should not dominate the ground floor unless you clearly mark it as a conservative reconstruction branch

The ground floor should not imply that every room is equally dangerous or equally secure.

## WHAT TO DO WITH WHIN AND ELODIN EVIDENCE
Use these carefully:

- **Whin’s room** proves that specialized padded accommodation exists, but because Whin is explicitly on the **second floor east**, do not place his named room on the ground floor as canon.
- **Elodin’s old copper-doored room** proves extreme containment exists somewhere in Haven, but do not force that exact room onto the ground floor unless shown as a highly uncertain reconstructed secure branch.

If you include either logic on the ground floor:
- segregate it
- label it reconstructed
- keep it secondary to the main manor-institution front

## DESIGN BRIDGE
Use this as the main reconstruction bridge:

A **large formal entry block** with a **reception / ledger desk** and **broad stair** feeds into **one or more long white ward corridors**, with at least one **more controlled side branch or secure wing** that remains modest and clearly marked if uncertain.

This bridge is plausible, canon-friendly, and should govern the composition.

## WINDOWS
Unlike the Archives, Haven **does have windows**.
The books support:
- high exterior windows
- stained-glass windows in the entryway

So windows are allowed where justified, especially in:
- the entry hall
- exterior ward walls
- formal front rooms

Do **not** overdo them into decorative cathedral glazing.
Keep them functional and institutionally elegant.

## HARD BANS
Do not include:
- Masters’ offices
- faculty governance chambers
- classrooms
- tiny clinic scale
- prison-block monotony replacing the formal manor front
- dungeon maze logic replacing the corridor system
- fantasy torture spaces
- ornamental gothic horror features
- elaborate underground prison levels
- over-named lore rooms with no textual support

## UNCERTAINTY HANDLING
If a detail is unknown:
- keep it generic
- keep it modest
- keep it functional
- label it reconstructed where appropriate

If a secure wing is uncertain:
- mark it reconstructed
- keep it compact
- avoid overpopulating it with named special rooms

If a new detail would force contradiction:
- omit it
- simplify it
- do not improvise

## GRAPHIC CONVENTIONS
Use:
- thick wall line for major outer walls
- lighter lines for internal partitions
- clear door swings where useful
- broad stair clearly shown
- windows only where justified
- simple labels
- plain key:
  - canon
  - implied
  - reconstructed

Suggested labels:
- entry hall
- desk
- broad stair
- ward
- supervision
- service
- secure corridor
- safer room
- stores

Do not over-label speculative details.

## SCOPE CONTROL
Show only:
- Haven ground floor
- minimal exterior edge needed to explain the entry
- the main arrival block
- the broad stair
- the ward corridors and differentiated care logic
- minimal support rooms

Do **not** show:
- the whole University
- the whole Haven grounds
- the second floor
- Whin’s exact room as ground-floor canon
- Elodin’s exact old room as ground-floor canon
- decorative landscape scenes

## FINAL IMAGE CHARACTER
The final image should feel like:
- a large University asylum with real funding behind it
- an institutional manor adapted for care and confinement
- eerily quiet and controlled
- formal at the front, more managed beyond
- technically useful as a floor-plan reference

It should not feel like:
- a fantasy madhouse
- a gothic dungeon
- a tiny medical cottage
- a palace state room
- a fully solved prison architecture diagram

## FINAL QUALITY CHECK
Before finalizing, verify:
- the entry hall is large and impressive
- stained-glass / vaulted / marble-front logic is preserved
- the desk is present in the front zone
- the broad stair is present
- long white corridor logic is clearly represented
- the building feels large enough for hundreds of patients
- room types are differentiated
- specialized containment remains segregated and cautious
- Whin is not wrongly treated as a ground-floor canon room
- the plan stays Rothfuss-accurate, not sensationalized

Generate **one single top-down architectural floor plan image only**.

Haven Level 2

Create a top-down floor plan for Haven (the Rookery / the Crockery),
Second Floor, from the Kingkiller Chronicle as a canon-constrained
reconstruction.

Canon (must not contradict):
- Haven's second floor is the principal INMATE FLOOR. All named inmate
  rooms in canon sit here, not on the ground floor (NOTW Ch 46).
- Inmates are numbered by wing and floor. Canon example: Alder Whin,
  an arcanist giller to Elodin, is held in "Second floor east, 247"
  (NOTW Ch 46).
- The floor connects to the ground floor by the WIDE MARBLE STAIRCASE
  rising from the grand entryway. This is the canonical route up and
  the top of the stair must land here.
- Circulation is a system of long, interconnected hallways linking
  distinct wings: a main EAST WING holding standard padded rooms, and
  a DIFFERENT WING holding specialist arcanist containment cells.
  Elodin leads Kvothe "through a long series of hallways to a
  different wing" to reach his former cell (NOTW Ch 46). Wings are
  connected but not directly adjacent.
- The floor reads as an INSTITUTIONAL ASYLUM, not a hospital. Sounds
  throughout the floor: moans, weeping, incessant chattering,
  screaming — "very faint" through the thick doors (NOTW Ch 46).

East Wing — standard padded rooms (canon):
- A LONG, WHITE HALLWAY lined with wooden doors (NOTW Ch 46).
- Marble floor, smooth enough that a man in stocking feet can slide
  on it (NOTW Ch 46).
- Each door is heavy timber with a SLIDING PANEL AT EYE LEVEL for
  observation, and a latch lockable from outside with a handler's key
  (NOTW Ch 46).
- Standard room (canon, as seen in Room 247):
  - Sizable bed.
  - Table with chairs.
  - TALL WINDOWS admit daylight — windows ARE canonical for inmate
    rooms, unlike the Archives.
  - Walls, ceiling, AND floor padded with thick white cloth,
    muffling sound.
- Restraint rooms are INTERSPERSED within the same corridor — Elodin
  wrenches open "the nearest door" off the main hallway and reveals
  a young man thrashing against LEATHER RESTRAINTS bound at wrist,
  waist, neck, and ankle. So restraint rooms share the corridor with
  standard padded rooms and are identifiable only by the straps
  fixed to the bed, not by different doors or room shell.

Different Wing — specialist arcanist containment (canon):
- Reached from the East Wing via "several shorter hallways" — not
  immediately adjacent to the ward corridor.
- Notable specimen: the COPPER DOOR cell (Elodin's former cell).
  Canon details:
  - Door entirely of copper. No handle on the inside. Lockable only
    from outside.
  - Walls and ceiling are BARE GREY STONE — NOT padded (contrast to
    East Wing).
  - Windows are thicker than usual, with faint reddish streaks
    (copper threads) running through the glass. Window frames are
    copper.
  - Interior air feels heavier, a pressure on the ears — the cell is
    built to SUPPRESS NAMING and sympathy, not sound.
  - Smells of lavender and fresh linen.
  - Furnished to a significantly higher standard than the East Wing:
    four-post bed with curtains, overstuffed couch, empty bookcase,
    large table with chairs.
  - Sits on an exterior wall. A BALCONY is visible through the
    windows but unreachable from inside (no door). Beyond the cell
    wall, a flat piece of roof sits ~20 feet above the gardens —
    canon confirms this wing is on the SECOND FLOOR (NOTW Ch 46).
- The building-wide containment logic applies on this floor most
  visibly: walls use "all-meshed stone" and wards on doors and
  windows (NOTW Ch 44, Simmon). The copper-door cell is an extreme
  example of the general security principle.

Staff presence (canon):
- Handlers patrol the hallways. Canon example: Timothy Generoy —
  huge, broad-shouldered, carries a hardback (writing board), holds a
  ring of keys, handles lockings and unlockings. He can be rebuked
  by a master but normally acts with institutional authority.

Reconstructed programme for the second floor (flag clearly; do not
render as canon):
- Number and orientation of wings: canon names East clearly; the
  "different wing" is canon-underdetermined. Pick a direction (e.g.
  West or South), label it reconstructed, and keep it consistent with
  any future Haven prompts.
- A handler's station near the top of the marble staircase — a
  supervised entry point to the ward corridors. Plausible at this
  scale (300+ inmates) but not named in-text.
- A linen / laundry drop, a medicine store, and a small handler break
  room along the service spine. Leave specific functions generic
  (service, supervision, store) rather than inventing named
  lore.
- A small number of leather-restraint rooms clearly marked along the
  East Wing corridor — canon shows they share doors with standard
  padded rooms, but for plan clarity flag a couple as "restraint
  fitted" in the reconstructed key.
- Quieter, higher-grade rooms in the specialist wing alongside the
  copper-door cell — other arcanist containment configurations that
  are not as extreme.
- Stairs down to the ground floor (canon: the grand marble stair).
- Stairs up to the third floor and beyond — Haven is taller than two
  storeys, but canon does not directly describe the upper storeys.
  Show a stair only; do not invent rooms above.

Hard bans:
- no Masters' offices
- no faculty governance chamber
- no ground-floor material (entryway, front desk, reception) — those
  are on the ground floor
- no windowless shell — inmate rooms canonically have tall windows
- no copper doors, copper-threaded glass, or bare grey stone cells
  anywhere OUTSIDE the specialist wing — that architecture is
  reserved for arcanist containment
- no padded rooms inside the specialist wing — padding is East Wing
  only
- no dungeon maze, castle keep, or fortress interior
- no open central atrium, courtyard, or light-well cutting through
  this floor
- no modern hospital cues (nurse stations, crash carts, triage
  boards, clipboards-on-walls, wheelchairs)
- no named inmate rooms BEYOND canon — use generic numbers in the
  pattern "2E-xxx" for east wing, "2W-xxx" or similar for the
  specialist wing, and clearly flag Room 247 in the East Wing as
  canon

Drawing style:
architectural floor plan
black and white
clear walls, doors, stairs, corridors, windows
room numbers in plain text (e.g. 247)
north arrow
scale bar
no people
no decorative fantasy elements

Rules:
- Respect the three-layer hierarchy: ward corridor (East Wing) ->
  connecting hallways -> specialist containment wing.
- The copper-door cell must sit against an exterior wall with a
  balcony outside, on the garden side of the building.
- Draw hallway lengths long enough to read as "long" — not short
  corridors; the canon wording is "long series of hallways".
- Keep the East Wing layout straight and monotonous — the repetition
  of identical doors is the point.
- Label each element canon, implied, or reconstructed in a
  visible map key.

Consistency requirements:
- Keep north aligned with the master campus map.
- Keep scale consistent with the approved building shell: manor-house
  footprint, ~500-bed institutional capacity.
- The top of the marble staircase on this floor must align vertically
  with the bottom of that staircase on the approved ground-floor
  plan.
- The specialist wing's exterior wall must align vertically with the
  garden elevation so the 20-foot balcony drop reads correctly.
- Preserve previously approved entrances, corridors, stairs, and
  adjacent rooms.
- Do not contradict earlier approved plans.
- If a new detail would force a contradiction, reject the detail
  rather than improvising.

Create a top-down floor plan for Hollows, Ground Floor, from the
Kingkiller Chronicle as a canon-constrained reconstruction.

Canon (must not contradict):
- Hollows is a simple, square stone building. Short profile — at least
  one full storey lower than neighbouring buildings like Mains
  (NOTW Ch 36). Two storeys plus a basement is the consistent
  reading.
- The building is reached from the Archives by going "down, then left"
  from the Archives entrance (NOTW Ch 36). Implies Hollows sits on
  slightly lower grade than the Archives, with an approach path from
  the Archives side.
- The front (approach) face is identifiable by:
  - STAINED GLASS windows depicting Teccam in a classic pose:
    standing BAREFOOT at the mouth of his cave, speaking to a GROUP
    of students. This is a multi-figure narrative window, not a
    portrait (NOTW Ch 36).
  - TWO BIG MAPLE TREES directly out front (NOTW Ch 36).
- Interior register: thick stone walls; shadowy and cool; a noticeable
  change from outside bright sunlight (NOTW Ch 85 — Kvothe on exit:
  "The bright afternoon sunlight was a shock after the shadowy cool
  of Hollows").

Ground-floor rooms (canon):
- ADMISSIONS THEATRE. An interior room configured as a formal
  theatre:
  - A RAISED STAGE at one end — the candidate climbs UP onto it
    (NOTW Ch 36) and stands at the edge of a wide circle of light.
  - The room is otherwise DARK; the only bright light is the wide
    circle around the masters' table.
  - The masters sit at a HUGE CRESCENT-SHAPED TABLE, itself RAISED
    on a dais, so even seated they look DOWN on the candidate.
  - Seating for observers — other students / staff CAN sit in the
    unlit portion and watch interviews (Kvothe overhears
    "hundreds of questions and thousands of answers" after
    sneaking in). The room is large enough to accommodate this.
- MASTERS' HALL. A separate, large WINDOWLESS room also containing a
  crescent-shaped table (NOTW Ch 40). Used for trials, grievances,
  and formal business, not admissions. Differences from the
  admissions theatre:
  - Crescent table is NOT raised on a dais; masters and accused sit
    close to eye level.
  - A smaller side table and chair are set for a minutes-clerk
    (Jamison in-text) at one side.
  - Room is explicitly windowless.
- ELODIN'S OFFICE (canon — NOTW Ch 46: "He had an office in Hollows,
  but never seemed to use it"). A master's personal office. Small,
  neglected. Its presence confirms that Hollows holds master offices
  generally, not just ceremonial space.
- BACK-OF-HOUSE SERVICE CORRIDOR. A back entrance with a lock exists
  (NOTW Ch 36 — Kvothe "snuck into Hollows through a back entrance,
  acting the part of an errand boy"). After the back entrance, TWO
  SEPARATE LOCKED DOORS sit between it and observation access to the
  admissions theatre — Kvothe picked both. The service side is
  layered, not a single-door back-of-house.

Not on this floor (canon or implied, belongs elsewhere):
- THE BURSAR. The Bursar (Riem) keeps his counting room and back room
  in the BASEMENT of Hollows (WMF Ch 89, Ch 93). Show stairs
  descending from the ground floor to the basement. Do not place the
  counting room on the ground floor.
- Additional master offices may live on the upper floor — canon names
  only Elodin's. Show stairs up; do not populate the upper floor on
  this plan.

Reconstructed programme (flag clearly; do not render as canon):
- Whether the admissions theatre and Masters' Hall are TWO SEPARATE
  ROOMS or ONE reconfigurable room is canon-underdetermined. Kvothe
  calls the Masters' Hall table "familiar" (meaning the same CRESCENT
  SHAPE, not necessarily the same furniture). For a floor plan, draw
  them as TWO SEPARATE ROOMS — one with a raised stage and dais
  (admissions theatre), one windowless and at ground level (Masters'
  Hall) — and flag the choice as reconstructed.
- An entry vestibule / lobby behind the front (stained-glass) doors.
- A small reception or runner's station inside the vestibule (canon
  shows messengers like Jamison's runner boys moving in and out of
  Hollows routinely).
- A short corridor or gallery linking the vestibule to the admissions
  theatre and to the Masters' Hall.
- One or two additional unnamed master offices (plausible — canon
  confirms offices exist, but names only Elodin's).
- Two stair positions: one down to the basement (Bursar), one up to
  the second floor. Position them consistent with any upper-floor or
  basement plans.

Hard bans:
- no Elodin's lecture hall, slate, or stepped-down seating — that is
  in MAINS, not Hollows
- no windowless exterior shell — Hollows has canonical stained glass
  on the approach face; the windowless-interior rule applies ONLY to
  the Masters' Hall inside
- no second stained-glass face unless justified (canon describes one
  approach face; do not invent more)
- no palace forecourt, ceremonial axis, or monumental stair approach
  — the front has two maples and stained glass, not a plaza
- no fortress, dungeon, or castle exterior
- no Archives-style severity (windowless block) — Hollows is the
  ceremonial and administrative face of the University
- no cathedral nave, altar, or religious program — Teccam iconography
  is SCHOLARLY (philosopher at cave mouth teaching students), not
  ecclesiastical
- no master governance chamber beyond the Masters' Hall itself — do
  not invent a separate "faculty meeting room"
- no direct public route into the admissions theatre that bypasses
  the layered security on the back-of-house side
- no Bursar's office, counting room, or treasury on the ground floor
  — that is BASEMENT material

Drawing style:
architectural floor plan
black and white
clear walls, doors, stairs, corridors, windows (stained glass on the
  front face labelled)
room labels
north arrow
scale bar
no people
no furniture unless architecturally relevant (raised dais and stage
  in admissions theatre ARE architecturally relevant; show them)
no decorative fantasy elements

Rules:
- Respect the three-register logic of the floor:
  - The CEREMONIAL front — stained glass, vestibule, admissions
    theatre — facing the Archives approach.
  - The ADMINISTRATIVE middle — Masters' Hall, master offices,
    circulation.
  - The SERVICE back — locked back entrance, service corridor,
    layered locked internal doors, stairs to basement.
- The admissions theatre must show: a raised stage, a raised crescent
  dais, and observation seating in the unlit area.
- The Masters' Hall must be drawn windowless, with an internal
  position (not against an exterior wall carrying windows).
- The back entrance and the admissions-theatre observation area must
  be separated by AT LEAST TWO locked door thresholds — this is
  canon.
- Keep room count modest. Do not add rooms just to fill space. Leave
  undefined area as service, storage, or corridor rather than
  inventing named lore.
- Label each element canon, implied, or reconstructed in a
  visible map key.

Consistency requirements:
- Keep north aligned with the master campus map.
- The front face (stained glass, two maples) must face toward the
  Archives approach — consistent with "down, then left" from the
  Archives entrance as shown on the approved site plan.
- Keep scale consistent with the approved building shell (short
  square footprint, smaller than Mains, distinctly smaller than the
  Archives).
- Preserve previously approved entrances, corridors, stairs, and
  adjacent rooms.
- Stair to basement on this plan must align vertically with the
  Bursar's counting room on the basement plan (when that plan is
  drawn).
- Do not contradict earlier approved plans.
- If a new detail would force a contradiction, reject the detail
  rather than improvising.

Thought for 2m 32s

Create a top-down floor plan for Hollows, Basement, from the Kingkiller
Chronicle as a canon-constrained reconstruction.

Canon (must not contradict):
- Hollows has a basement level, reached by a flight of stairs from the
  ground floor inside the building. Lorren leads Kvothe "into another
  stone building, through a hallway, and down a flight of stairs"
  to reach the bursar (NOTW Ch 37). WMF confirms this stone building
  is Hollows: "I paid a visit to the bursar in the basement of
  Hollows" (WMF Ch 89) and "the basement of Hollows where the bursar
  kept his counting room" (WMF Ch 93).
- The BURSAR'S COUNTING ROOM is the principal canonical space on this
  floor. The bursar (Riem in-text) works here. Balding, pinch-faced,
  irritable; not in the habit of giving money out; keeps close
  control of the University's cash (NOTW Ch 37, WMF Ch 89, Ch 93).
- The counting room has a BACK ROOM opening off it. Riem "disappeared
  into his back room and returned in a moment with an envelope of
  thick paper" (WMF Ch 93) — the back room is where tuition
  envelopes and cash reserves are stored, not a simple cupboard.
- Access workflow at the counter:
  - Student (or master) arrives at the counter in the counting room
    with a tuition slip or payment instruction.
  - Riem checks the slip, then passes INTO the back room to retrieve
    the cash or envelope.
  - Transaction completed at the counter; student leaves.
- Transactions are two-way: the bursar receives tuition payments AND
  pays out negative tuitions (Kvothe's first term, NOTW Ch 37) and
  envelopes of cash / tuition slips (WMF Ch 93). The counting room
  must be built to handle both directions securely.
- The interior is down a flight of stairs from a ground-floor
  hallway in Hollows (NOTW Ch 37). Stone construction throughout,
  consistent with the rest of Hollows.

Reconstructed programme (flag clearly; do not render as canon):
- A secure STRONGROOM / VAULT ROOM separate from the back room.
  Plausible for a University the size of fifteen hundred students
  with 300+ inmates at Haven, a Medica, and ongoing staff payroll.
  The bursar cannot reasonably keep all working cash and records in
  a single unsecured back room. Flag as reconstructed.
- A RECORDS ROOM / LEDGER STORE. Tuition ledgers, student debt
  records, and accounting books. Plausible given the scale of
  University business. Not named in canon; flag as reconstructed.
- A small BURSAR'S OFFICE (distinct from the counting room counter)
  where Riem does paperwork out of the public view. Implied but not
  named.
- A PUBLIC WAITING AREA or short queue space at the foot of the
  stairs before the counting-room counter. Implied at this scale;
  reconstructed.
- A locked SERVICE CORRIDOR connecting the basement back-of-house
  to the ground-floor service corridor of Hollows (allowing staff
  movement without crossing the public counter).
- Cold storage / supply rooms on the service side — stone cellar
  space under a stone building is the obvious use. Keep generic
  (storage, service) rather than named.
- Stair up to the ground floor — its position must align with the
  basement-stair position on the approved ground-floor plan.

Access and security logic (canon + implied):
- ONE controlled descent from the ground floor — the canonical public
  route to the counting room.
- At least ONE service route for staff (implied — a purely public
  single stair would force staff and cash to share the counter
  traffic, which is operationally implausible for a bursar).
- The counting-room counter itself is the public threshold; past the
  counter is staff-only.
- The back room is reached from behind the counter, not from the
  public side.
- If a strongroom is drawn, it should sit BEHIND the back room — a
  two-door depth between the public and the cash.

Hard bans:
- no windows — this is a basement under a stone building; lightwells
  or exterior windows would contradict the basement reading
- no natural light of any kind; lighting is sympathy lamps or
  candles / oil only (keep light sources off the plan unless the
  fixture position matters architecturally)
- no grand stair, marble floor, or ceremonial architecture — this is
  a working counting room, not a treasury of state
- no ornamental treatment — the space is utilitarian
- no Masters' Hall, admissions theatre, master offices, or Elodin's
  office — those are GROUND-FLOOR material
- no connection to the Underthing, tunnels, or subterranean networks
  (the Underthing connects to the Archives and Mains, not Hollows;
  do not invent such a connection)
- no dungeon, cells, detention, or punitive spaces — Hollows is
  administrative, not custodial; custody belongs to Haven
- no armoury or guard room — the University does not canonically
  keep armed guards
- no vaulted ceremonial crypt, catacomb, or mausoleum atmospherics —
  keep the register institutional and plain

Drawing style:
architectural floor plan
black and white
clear walls, doors, stairs, corridors
counter line at the bursar's counting-room public threshold
room labels
north arrow
scale bar
no people
no furniture unless architecturally relevant (counter, door
  positions, and stair landings are architecturally relevant)
no decorative fantasy elements

Rules:
- Respect the three-register logic of the floor:
  - PUBLIC — stair down, short waiting area, counting-room counter.
  - STAFF — back room, bursar's office, records, optional strongroom.
  - SERVICE — service corridor and ancillary storage.
- Keep the plan compact. Hollows is a short square building; the
  basement cannot exceed the ground-floor footprint.
- Do not invent named rooms to fill space. Leave undefined area as
  storage, service, or unlabelled.
- The counting room and its back room must read as the largest and
  most developed spaces on this floor.
- Label each element canon, implied, or reconstructed in a
  visible map key.

Consistency requirements:
- Keep north aligned with the master campus map.
- Basement footprint must not exceed the Hollows ground-floor
  footprint.
- The stair up to the ground floor on this plan must align

  add a second public route in or out of the basement that bypasses

- If a new detail would force a contradiction, reject the detail
  rather than improvising.

Create a top-down floor plan for Hollows, Second Floor, from the
Kingkiller Chronicle as a canon-constrained reconstruction.

Canon (must not contradict):
- Hollows is a simple, square stone building with a short profile —
  lower than Mains, distinctly smaller than the Archives (NOTW
  Ch 36). Two storeys plus a basement is the consistent reading.
- This plan is the second (upper) storey. Ground floor holds the
  admissions theatre, Masters' Hall, Elodin's office, entry
  vestibule, and back-of-house service circulation. Basement holds
  the Bursar's counting room and back room. All of those belong on
  OTHER plans, not this one.
- Elodin has a named office in Hollows that he "never seemed to
  use" (NOTW Ch 46). Canon places his office somewhere in Hollows
  but does not specify floor. For the pack as a whole, place
  Elodin's office on the GROUND FLOOR (where foot traffic reaches
  it) so the second floor can hold other master offices without
  confusion. Do not place Elodin's office on this plan.
- Canon names no specific rooms on the second floor of Hollows. The
  existence of the second floor is implied by the "short building"
  silhouette (two storeys plus basement) and by the need to house
  the University's administrative apparatus somewhere. Everything
  on this plan is therefore IMPLIED or RECONSTRUCTED — the map key
  must reflect this honestly.
- Interior register: thick stone walls; shadowy and cool (NOTW
  Ch 85). Lit entirely by sympathy lamps.
- No stained glass on this floor's windows. The Teccam stained-glass
  composition is on the front (ground-floor) face only — it depicts
  a narrative scene meant to be read at eye level on approach, not
  a repeated motif up the façade (NOTW Ch 36). Upper-floor windows
  are plain leaded glass or sympathy-lamp lit interior rooms.

Implied programme (flag clearly; not canon):
- MASTER OFFICES. Canon confirms master offices exist in Hollows
  (Elodin's); the University has nine masters. Several master
  offices on this floor is the cleanest reading. Keep the count
  modest — four to six offices, not nine, since some masters
  canonically work elsewhere (Kilvin in the Fishery workshop,
  Arwyl in the Medica, Lorren in the Archives, Mandrag in the
  alchemy complex). Plausible occupants of Hollows offices:
  Chancellor (Herma), Master Rhetorician (Hemme), Master
  Arithmetician (Brandeur), Master Linguist, plus one or two
  unnamed. Label offices by TITLE, not by current occupant
  (Chancellor's Office, not "Herma's Office") — masters change.
- CHANCELLOR'S OFFICE. If placed on this floor, should be the
  largest and most privately sited. The Chancellor runs admissions
  and presides at the Masters' Hall; a dedicated office makes sense.
- CLERK'S ROOM / ADMINISTRATIVE OFFICE. Jamison oversees "the
  kitchens, the laundry, the stables, and the stockrooms" (NOTW
  Ch 40) and manages runner boys. He needs a work space. Small;
  plain; near the stair landing so runners can reach him.
- RECORDS ROOM. Student records, admissions ledgers, grievance
  minutes taken by Jamison during trials (NOTW Ch 40). Adjacent to
  the clerk's room is the parsimonious placement.
- SMALL MEETING ROOM. A working room for informal master
  consultations (distinct from the Masters' Hall below, which is
  the formal trial / grievance space). Plausible; keep small.
- A circulation corridor spine connecting offices to the stair
  landing. Keep straight and simple — Hollows is a short square
  building, not a labyrinth.

Reconstructed programme (flag clearly; render lightly):
- A small WAITING AREA / BENCH near the stair landing for students
  summoned to a master's office.
- A SERVICE CLOSET or two for cleaning, sympathy-lamp replenishment,
  and paper stores.
- Stair down to the ground floor — position must align vertically
  with the stair up on the approved ground-floor plan.
- A back-of-house stair if the ground-floor service corridor has a
  matching upper run. Only include if the ground-floor plan shows
  one. Otherwise omit.
- An ATTIC ACCESS (hatch or small stair) is PLAUSIBLE but optional.
  Canon never describes a Hollows attic. Omit unless the building
  shell requires it.

Not on this floor (keep off the plan):
- Admissions theatre. Ground floor.
- Masters' Hall. Ground floor, windowless, with crescent table.
- Elodin's office. Ground floor per this pack's convention.
- Bursar's counting room, back room, any treasury or strongroom
  function. Basement.
- Entry vestibule, stained-glass front. Ground floor.
- Back-of-house locked-door sequence leading to admissions
  observation. Ground floor.
- Any Archives, Haven, Mains, Medica, or Fishery material. Other
  buildings.

Hard bans:
- no admissions theatre, no Masters' Hall, no crescent table, no
  raised dais on this floor
- no counting room, no back room, no treasury
- no lecture halls — Hollows does not contain teaching spaces;
  teaching is in Mains and the Medica
- no Elodin lecture hall / slate / stepped-down seating — that is
  Mains (WMF Ch 13)
- no stained glass on this floor — the canonical Teccam window is
  on the ground-floor front face only
- no palace state rooms, grand library, or galleried hall — Hollows
  is a working administrative building, not a great hall
- no dungeon, holding cell, or detention room — custody is Haven
- no armoury, guardroom, or fortified wing
- no cathedral / religious / monastic fittings — Teccam iconography
  is scholarly, not ecclesiastical
- no naming Elodin's specific office here (it is canon-placed in
  Hollows but this pack puts it on the ground floor; name unoccupied
  or title-labelled offices only)
- no more than SEVEN offices total on this floor — the University
  has nine masters but four of them work in other buildings

Drawing style:
architectural floor plan
black and white
clear walls, doors, stairs, corridors, windows (plain — no stained
  glass)
room labels by FUNCTION or TITLE, not by current occupant name
north arrow
scale bar
no people
no furniture unless architecturally relevant (desk positions may
  matter for a Chancellor's Office; keep minimal)
no decorative fantasy elements

Rules:
- Respect the administrative register of the floor. This is office
  work, not ceremony.
- Keep the plan compact. Hollows is a short square building; the
  second floor cannot exceed the ground-floor footprint.
- Keep room count modest. Canon names ZERO rooms on this floor; an
  overfilled plan would overstate what Rothfuss wrote.
- Leave undefined area as corridor, service, or unlabelled
  rather than inventing lore.
- Most rooms on this plan are IMPLIED or RECONSTRUCTED. The map key
  must make this visible — almost nothing here is canon.
- Label each element canon, implied, or reconstructed.

Consistency requirements:
- Keep north aligned with the master campus map.
- The second-floor footprint must match the Hollows building shell
  and must not exceed the ground-floor footprint.
- The stair down to the ground floor on this plan must align
  vertically with the stair up on the approved ground-floor plan.
- Exterior window positions must match the approved building shell
  as seen from outside. No new window openings that would change
  the façade.
- Preserve previously approved entrances, corridors, stairs, and
  adjacent rooms.
- Do not contradict earlier approved plans.
- If a new detail would force a contradiction, reject the detail
  rather than improvising.

Create a top-down architectural plan of the ADMISSIONS THEATRE inside
Hollows, Ground Floor, from the Kingkiller Chronicle as a
canon-constrained reconstruction. This is a single-room plan, not a
whole-floor plan — the drawing should show this room and its
immediate threshold doors only.

Canon (must not contradict):
- The Admissions Theatre is located on the ground floor of Hollows
  (NOTW Ch 36). Hollows is a simple, square stone building with a
  short profile; the theatre is one of the two major ceremonial
  rooms on this floor, alongside the Masters' Hall (which is a
  separate room and belongs on the Hollows ground-floor plan, not
  this one).
- The room functions as an empty theatre configured for formal
  admissions interviews. A single candidate stands in it at a time;
  the nine masters question the candidate; observation from the
  darkened seating is possible (NOTW Ch 36).
- The room is explicitly called "an empty theater" (NOTW Ch 36).
  Draw it as a theatre, not a courtroom, not a chapel, not a lecture
  hall.

Canonical architectural elements (must appear on the plan):
- A STAGE. Candidates "climb up onto" it (NOTW Ch 36), so the stage
  is a RAISED PLATFORM above the auditorium floor level. Access is
  by a short flight of steps from the auditorium side.
- A RAISED DAIS at the back of the stage carrying the MASTERS'
  CRESCENT TABLE. The dais is elevated ABOVE the stage so that even
  seated, the masters "look down" on the candidate (NOTW Ch 36).
  So the room has TWO levels of elevation: auditorium floor →
  stage → dais.
- THE CRESCENT TABLE. Huge, crescent-shaped (U-shaped with a curved
  back), seating NINE masters. Canonical seating:
  - Chancellor (Master Linguist) in the centre.
  - Master Rhetorician immediately LEFT of the Chancellor (Hemme
    in-text).
  - Master Namer immediately RIGHT of the Chancellor (Elodin
    in-text).
  - Master Arithmetician at one END of the crescent (Brandeur
    in-text).
  - The remaining five masters (Archivist, Physicker, Alchemist,
    Artificer, Sympathist) distributed along the table; exact
    positions canon-underdetermined (reconstructed).
  - Label seats by TITLE, not by current occupant.
- A WIDE CIRCLE OF LIGHT falling on the dais and the front of the
  stage. Produced by focused overhead sympathy lamps (NOT daylight,
  NOT candlelight). Mark the lit circle on the plan as a dashed
  boundary — the candidate "stands at the edge of the light"
  (NOTW Ch 36).
- The rest of the room is DARK. Auditorium seating and observation
  areas must read as unlit.
- TIERED AUDITORIUM SEATING surrounding the stage on at least the
  front. Observation is canon: Kvothe watched "hundreds of
  questions and thousands of answers" from inside the room after
  sneaking in (NOTW Ch 36). Seat count must support several dozen
  observers, not hundreds — this is a controlled observation space,
  not a public amphitheatre.

Access and circulation (canon):
- The ROOM HAS A BACK ENTRANCE. It is used for service / errand
  access (Kvothe snuck in "acting the part of an errand boy" —
  NOTW Ch 36). Lockable.
- Between the back entrance and the observation seats there are AT
  LEAST TWO SEPARATE LOCKED DOOR THRESHOLDS. Kvothe explicitly
  picked two locks to get from the back entrance to an observation
  position (NOTW Ch 36). Draw both door thresholds on the plan.
- The MASTERS HAVE A SEPARATE ENTRANCE onto the dais. When Kvothe
  arrives, the masters are already seated and talking among
  themselves (NOTW Ch 36); they did not enter past him. Their
  circulation connects the dais directly to their offices / the
  Masters' Hall via a staff-only route that bypasses the auditorium.
- The CANDIDATE ENTRANCE is a separate, public-facing door used by
  the student being interviewed. Opens onto the auditorium floor,
  with a clear line of sight to the stage steps.

Reconstructed programme (flag clearly; do not render as canon):
- The exact SHAPE of the auditorium seating — straight rows, curved
  rows, or raked tiers — is not specified. Draw raked semicircular
  rows focused on the stage as the most plausible theatre-register
  reading.
- A small waiting alcove near the candidate entrance where the next
  interviewee stands before being called forward. Implied at this
  scale; reconstructed.
- A narrow behind-the-dais vestibule where masters enter, remove
  outer cloaks if needed, and take their seats. Reconstructed.
- Exact POSITION of the overhead sympathy lamps creating the circle
  of light. Canon requires the light; the fixture arrangement is
  reconstruction. Keep minimal.
- The internal height of the room — plausibly higher than standard
  ground-floor rooms to accommodate the tiered seating and overhead
  lamps. Not drawn on a plan, but note it in a margin comment if
  any elevation detail is needed.

Not in this room (keep off the plan; belongs elsewhere on the
ground floor):
- The Masters' Hall and its own crescent table — a separate
  windowless room for trials and grievances.
- Elodin's office.
- The entry vestibule and Teccam stained-glass front face.
- The service corridor spine running along the back-of-house of
  Hollows — show only the two locked doors where it meets this
  room's back entrance.

Hard bans:
- no windows in the Admissions Theatre itself — the room must be
  dark except for the circle of light; exterior windows would
  contradict the controlled-lighting condition (a back wall abutting
  the Hollows interior is the cleanest reading)
- no daylight of any kind inside the room
- no candles, torches, chandeliers, or flame fixtures — the
  University's standard lighting is sympathy lamps
- no lecture hall form with a single slate at the front — that is
  Elodin's lecture hall in MAINS (WMF Ch 13), not this room
- no courtroom dock, witness box, or jury benches — this is a
  theatre for admissions, not a trial space (trials happen in the
  Masters' Hall)
- no chapel, altar, pulpit, or religious fittings — Teccam
  iconography is scholarly and is on the exterior front face of
  Hollows, not inside this room
- no single-entrance plan — canon requires at least THREE
  circulation routes (candidate, masters, back-of-house service
  with observation access)
- no direct unlocked path from the back entrance to the observation
  seats — the two-lock sequence is canon
- no grand tiered amphitheatre with hundreds of seats — the
  observation capacity is dozens, not hundreds
- no decorative proscenium arch, theatre curtain, or stage flats —
  the room is "empty," institutional, scholarly, not performative

Drawing style:
architectural floor plan
black and white
clear walls, doors, stairs, steps, thresholds
elevation levels marked (auditorium floor / stage / dais)
dashed boundary for the circle of light
crescent table drawn in plan with nine seat positions, each
  labelled by master title
circulation routes drawn with arrows or dashed lines for candidate,
  masters, and back-of-house
room label
north arrow (consistent with master campus map and the approved
  Hollows ground-floor plan)
scale bar
no people
no decorative fantasy elements

Rules:
- Respect the THREE-ELEVATION logic: auditorium floor, stage (raised),
  dais (further raised). All three must be clearly visible in plan.
- Respect the THREE-CIRCULATION logic: candidate route from front;
  masters' route from behind the dais; back-of-house / observation
  route through two locked thresholds.
- The crescent table must be drawn in its canonical curve, opening
  toward the candidate's position. Chancellor's seat in the centre.
- Seat labels use TITLE (Master Archivist, Master Physicker, etc.),
  never current occupant name.
- Label each element canon, implied, or reconstructed in a
  visible map key.

Consistency requirements:
- Keep north aligned with the master campus map and with the
  approved Hollows ground-floor plan.
- Room footprint, wall positions, and door positions must match the
  Admissions Theatre as it appears on the approved Hollows
  ground-floor plan.
- The back-entrance / two-lock sequence drawn here must align with
  the service-corridor position on the approved Hollows ground-
  floor plan.
- The masters' rear entrance must align with a corridor on the
  ground-floor plan that connects to the Masters' Hall and / or
  master offices.
- Do not contradict earlier approved plans.
- If a new detail would force a contradiction, reject the detail
  rather than improvising.
